3. **The hint.** The user chose the words on the "Best all-0 path" heading: "The best path that activates at the first chance every time (no skips). Every squeeze and early fill on it is 0 ms or easier." This replaces D66's "It needs no timing." The User Guide says the same.
4. **Every analyzed chart reads Stale.** The user chose "Mark all Stale". The results stamp becomes "2.1.0+allzero", a second bump inside the unreleased 2.1.0, so results saved by earlier 2.1.0 builds are analyzed again.
On the 97 test charts, 68 have an all-0 path again, up from 61 on main, and every chart's all-0 list (each path and variant) is identical to the one before E3 (0cbe9bd's parent). Seven charts get theirs back: Feast of Fire, Tapped Out, Sugar/Tzu, The Sentinel, Limb From Limb, YYZ and I Am... All Of Me, the seven D60 listed. Six more swap E3's lower path back to 2.0's higher one, which has a squeeze-out exactly on the SP end: Saying Sorry, I'm A Believer, Act One Scene One, Book, All These People and Snakeskin Boots. Slander at Expert, Pro Drums, 2x Bass, cap 4, 4 scores and a 10 ms Path limit now shows `0 E0 0 0 0` at 486,740, against an optimal 503,720.

**D86 (the speed work's open calls), 2026-10-06.** The perf exploration (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-06-perf-exploration/REPORT.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-06-perf-exploration/REPORT.md`)) left six calls for the user, and planning added a seventh. Each was explained with where its number came from, its cost and its alternatives, and the user took every recommendation:
1. **A failed group COMMIT retries each chart alone.** The batch writer saves up to 16 charts in one transaction, each in its own savepoint. If the group's COMMIT fails, the writer rolls the group back and saves each of its charts again in its own transaction. Only a chart whose own save fails is marked failed, so D71 item 6 holds exactly as before.
2. **A group holds at most 16 charts.** 16 and 64 measured the same (11.5 s against 11.7 s for a full run), because a group also closes whenever the writer catches up with the workers; groups average 10 to 13 charts. At about 0.5 ms per chart, 16 keeps the store lock and the progress lag near 8 ms, under one screen refresh.
3. **The WAL checkpoint moves to 10,000 pages only while a batch runs.** At batch end one checkpoint forces every result to disk and truncates `hydra.db-wal` to zero, and the setting goes back to SQLite's 1,000 pages. 10,000 pages of 4,096 bytes plus a 24-byte frame header each is 41.2 MB of log at most. The installed Hydra's log already stood at 45.6 MB (11,074 pages) that day, because SQLite reuses the log file without shrinking it. Measured after wave 1 merged (M1, 92bd322): the batch-end checkpoint took 433 ms on the whole library, under the plan's one-second line, so the progress strip needed no change.
4. **mimalloc gets a measured trial later, not a ship decision.** After the other speed changes land, an agent may download the mimalloc source release from github.com/microsoft/mimalloc into a throwaway worktree and measure it. Shipping it, a new third-party dependency, comes back to the user with numbers. The Windows segment heap (one manifest line) ships in the meantime.
5. **`build_cpp.ps1` builds with `--parallel`, uncapped.** Two cold builds took 83 s without it and 60 s with it, one run each. It changes build speed only.
6. **The user times a first-ever scan after their next reboot.** Another session read every song.ini in 44 s the first time and 0.9 s the second. The user runs one timed scan into a scratch database after a reboot, before opening Hydra, so the scan work knows whether first opens dominate.
7. **hydra_batch's per-chart lines come in bursts.** Under group commit, a chart's line prints only after its group is saved, so up to 16 lines can appear at once, about every 8 ms. Each line's text is unchanged. The user accepted this, because a line must never name a chart that isn't on disk yet.

The plan's "User decisions" block, copied word for word when the plan left the repo (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-06-perf-speedups.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-06-perf-speedups.md`)):

**User decisions (D86, `docs/audit/2026-10-03-fix-decisions.md`, 2026-10-06).** Quoted in full, because every number below comes from them:

1. "A failed group COMMIT retries each chart alone. The batch writer saves up to 16 charts in one transaction, each in its own savepoint. If the group's COMMIT fails, the writer rolls the group back and saves each of its charts again in its own transaction. Only a chart whose own save fails is marked failed, so D71 item 6 holds exactly as before."
2. "A group holds at most 16 charts. 16 and 64 measured the same (11.5 s against 11.7 s for a full run), because a group also closes whenever the writer catches up with the workers; groups average 10 to 13 charts. At about 0.5 ms per chart, 16 keeps the store lock and the progress lag near 8 ms, under one screen refresh."
3. "The WAL checkpoint moves to 10,000 pages only while a batch runs. At batch end one checkpoint forces every result to disk and truncates `hydra.db-wal` to zero, and the setting goes back to SQLite's 1,000 pages. 10,000 pages of 4,096 bytes plus a 24-byte frame header each is 41.2 MB of log at most. The installed Hydra's log already stood at 45.6 MB (11,074 pages) that day, because SQLite reuses the log file without shrinking it."
4. "mimalloc gets a measured trial later, not a ship decision. After the other speed changes land, an agent may download the mimalloc source release from github.com/microsoft/mimalloc into a throwaway worktree and measure it. Shipping it, a new third-party dependency, comes back to the user with numbers. The Windows segment heap (one manifest line) ships in the meantime."
5. "`build_cpp.ps1` builds with `--parallel`, uncapped. Two cold builds took 83 s without it and 60 s with it, one run each. It changes build speed only."
6. "The user times a first-ever scan after their next reboot. Another session read every song.ini in 44 s the first time and 0.9 s the second. The user runs one timed scan into a scratch database after a reboot, before opening Hydra, so the scan work knows whether first opens dominate."

Also binding: D71 item 6 (a failed save during a batch fails that chart alone and the batch goes on), D72 (a database that fails at startup or at a batch's start says so) and D73 (every store read throws instead of answering empty).

**D87 (hydra.db stores summaries only; details come from the engine when a song opens), 2026-10-07.** The storage audit (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-06-storage-audit-handoff.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-06-storage-audit-handoff.md`)) found that 338 MB of the installed database is almost all saved copies of engine output, and that the engine re-analyzes a chart in 1.8 ms typical, 12.8 ms at the 99th percentile and 294 ms at worst (Nirvana's "Endless, Nameless Setlist"). A 74-chart re-analysis reproduced the stored rows exactly. The user took the recommended answer to every question:
1. **Analyze on click.** hydra.db keeps the chart library, one summary row per chart and settings, and `meta`. Path nodes, path links, structure blobs, tempo maps, song names and lengths (`songmeta`) and kick dynamics are no longer stored. A click runs the engine's normal analysis on a worker thread. The library list, its sorting, filters and stars, the DM report and the fill comparison read the summary rows as before. `hydra_replay dump --no-analyze` and `hydra_batch --reindex` go away. Expected size: about 20 MB.
2. **A click saves the summary.** When a click's analysis finishes and the song's row is missing, Stale or different, the summary row is saved, so the library row and the Paths tab always show the same numbers.
3. **An edited chart is re-hashed on click.** The click runs the size-and-date check a rescan uses. If the file changed, it re-hashes it, updates that chart's library row, then analyzes the new file. This also fixes a bug: today the Paths tab shows the old file's paths as Ready, and Analyze saves the new file's result under the old file's hash.
4. **Rows of charts that left the library are deleted at each scan.** Rows of other chart modes for a chart still in the library are kept. This replaces D77 item 2: every result on a report now has a library row.
5. **The path report analyzes the library.** After a batch, and in `hydra_report`, the report runs the engine over every library chart with a Ready row, on all cores. Charts the batch just analyzed are reused. The report reads exactly as before. The extra time was estimated at 5 to 15 s and is measured before building; a worse number comes back to the user.
6. **The Analyze / Re-analyze button is removed.** A fast chart shows its paths with no progress box. A chart still analyzing after 0.15 s shows today's "Analyzing chart..." box with its Cancel button. The library's Stale tooltip ends "Click the song or run a batch to refresh it."
7. **The upgrade is in place, with no backup copy.** The first open of the new Hydra drops the detail tables and shrinks the file once. An older Hydra would find no path details in the upgraded file.
8. **The hydra_replay card ("Make hydra_replay use the engine, not a copy") runs as a parallel track.**
9. **Broken rules show today's warning.** With hydra_rules.ini broken, a click shows "Analysis is off until hydra_rules.ini is fixed and Hydra is restarted." and no paths. The library still shows every stored score and best path.
10. **No "Done!" flash after a click's analysis.** The progress box goes away and the paths appear.
11. **Cancel shows "Analysis cancelled." with a "Try again" button.** Nothing is saved for the cancelled song, and clicking another song starts that song's analysis.

The plan's "User decisions" block, copied word for word when the plan left the repo (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-summary-only-storage.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-summary-only-storage.md`)):

**User decisions (already made), D87, 2026-10-07:**
1. Analyze on click; store only the library, summary rows and `meta`.
2. A click saves the summary when the row is missing, Stale or different.
3. An edited chart is re-hashed on click, its library row is updated, and then it's analyzed.
4. Rows of charts that left the library are deleted at each scan. Other chart modes of a library chart are kept.
5. The path report analyzes the library on all cores and reuses the batch's charts. It reads exactly as before.
6. The Analyze / Re-analyze button is removed. The progress box appears only after 0.15 s. The Stale tooltip ends "Click the song or run a batch to refresh it."
7. Upgrade in place, no backup copy.
8. The hydra_replay card runs as a parallel track.
9. With broken rules, a click shows today's "Analysis is off..." warning and no paths.
10. No "Done!" flash after a click's analysis.
11. Cancel shows "Analysis cancelled." with a "Try again" button.

**D88 (mimalloc ships), 2026-10-07.** The mimalloc trial (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-07-mimalloc-trial.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-07-mimalloc-trial.md`); its numbers are in `docs/development.md`, "mimalloc trial numbers") found mimalloc v3.5.3 cut Hydra's own CPU work about 10% on a whole-library re-analysis, with no change in wall time and peak memory up from about 412 MB to 735 MB. The recommendation was not to ship it. The user decided to ship it anyway, because the CPU saving is real, and approved downloading the same source file again (`mimalloc-v3.5.3-source.tar.gz`, 1,458,292 bytes). Its source is vendored in `third_party/mimalloc`. Every shipped exe links its override DLL, and `mimalloc.dll` and `mimalloc-redirect.dll` install beside them. mimalloc's MIT licence ships with Hydra.

**D89 (three follow-ups from building D87), 2026-10-07.** Building D87's first wave raised three questions. The user took the recommended answer to each:
1. **The path report says when it left a chart out.** The report now analyzes each chart's file, so a file deleted or broken since the last scan can't be analyzed. When that happens, the page shows "Left out: N charts whose file couldn't be read." under its subtitle, with those charts' file paths listed under it. Nothing is shown when N is 0.
2. **hydra_report names the real cause on a database with no chart library.** A database that `hydra_batch` built with folder arguments has no chart library, and the report covers only library charts. There hydra_report prints "This database has no chart library. Run hydra_batch without folder arguments, or scan in Hydra, to build one." instead of "Nothing is analyzed under these settings".
3. **The video tools follow `hydra_replay dump`.** `dump` no longer takes `--db`. The main session updates `C:\Users\Patrick\Downloads\Hydra\video-tools` (its own repository) so `replay.py`, `fcvideo.py` and its README stop passing it, after the storage change merges.

**D90 (two details of analyze on click), 2026-10-07.** Building D87's click raised two display questions. The user took the recommended answer to both:
1. **A setting change re-analyzes the open song.** With a song open, changing a setting (2x Bass, the Path limit, the SP cap and the rest) analyzes it again under the new settings, the same as clicking it. Its summary row for those settings is saved when missing or different (D87 item 2). Other songs are untouched until clicked or batched.
2. **A click's analysis doesn't lock the settings bar.** The bar stays usable while a click analyzes. Changing a setting cancels that analysis and starts one under the new settings. The lock during a whole-library batch is unchanged.

**D91 (the library's not-analyzed tooltip), 2026-10-07.** With the Analyze button gone (D87 item 6), the library's tooltip on a row with no result told the user to press a button that no longer exists. The user chose the recommended wording, which matches the Stale tooltip: "Not analyzed yet. Click the song or run a batch to analyze it."

**D92 (names on the DM and fill pages), 2026-10-07.** With the stored song table gone (D87 item 1), a chart's name comes from the chart library, so a result whose chart the library doesn't list has no name. The user chose the path report's rule for the DM comparison page and hydra_fillcompare too: a database with no chart library stops with D89 item 2's sentence, "This database has no chart library. Run hydra_batch without folder arguments, or scan in Hydra, to build one.", and a result whose chart isn't in the library is left off the page. The app's own database is unchanged, since every result there is a library chart.

**D93 (the database's side file is capped at 4 MB), 2026-10-07.** While Hydra is open, SQLite collects writes in a side file next to the database (hydra.db-wal) before folding them in. By default that file stays at its largest size until Hydra closes, and a whole-library batch can leave it tens of MB. The user chose the recommended cap: every open sets `PRAGMA journal_size_limit` to 4194304 bytes (4 MB), so after each fold-in the side file shrinks back to at most 4 MB. Nothing in the app and no stored result changes.

**D94 (the Preview's changed-chart line), 2026-10-07.** With the Analyze button gone (D87 item 6), the Preview's line for a chart file that changed after the click analyzed it still said "Analyze it again to see its path." The user chose the recommended wording, in the same family as D87's Stale tooltip: "This chart changed since it was analyzed. Click the song again to see its path." The UserGuide quotes it the same way.

**D95 (memory: four calls from the memory audit), 2026-10-07.** The memory audit (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-07-memory-audit.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-07-memory-audit.md`)) left four questions. A purge-delay sweep that day, on a whole-library `--redo` of a copy of the installed database, measured mimalloc's peak commit at about 800 MB as shipped, 690 to 815 MB with a 250, 100 or 25 ms purge delay, 530 MB with 0 (about three times the kernel time), and 320 to 390 MB on the segment heap. With the default delay, about 450 MB was still committed when the run ended; with 100 ms or less, 16 MB. The user took the recommended answer to every question:
1. **mimalloc hands its kept memory back once when a batch ends.** One `mi_collect(true)` call runs when a library batch finishes. The purge delay stays at mimalloc's default and D88 stands.
2. **No cap on giant charts running side by side, for now.** The batch peak is measured again after the per-chord arrays are pre-sized (the audit's fix 2), and the question comes back only if giants still stack up.
3. **The Yu Gothic merge stays.** Every Japanese character in the library is already drawn by Shippori Antique B1, but dropping Yu Gothic would draw 12 rarer characters (such as ē, ō, ∞ and Ω) as the missing-character box in 24 charts. The font files are memory-mapped instead (the audit's fix 5), so Windows keeps only the pages it reads.
4. **The Preview frees its GPU render targets when it closes.** Reopening it re-creates them; the cost is measured to confirm it is a few ms.

D95 addendum, 2026-10-08 (code-only, under call 1): the path report build analyzes the library's charts the way a batch does, so it makes the same one `mi_collect(true)` call when it ends. Without it, the report memory run (D103 item 27's note) left about 766 MB committed after a warm build and about 295 MB after a cold one; with it, about 54 MB, of which about 42 MB is the report's rows. Nothing on screen or stored changes.

**D96 (a click saves a touched chart's size and time), 2026-10-07.** A chart file saved again with the same content keeps its hash, but its size-and-time fingerprint moves. The click noticed, hashed the file, found the same hash and saved nothing, so every later click hashed it again until a rescan. The user was asked whether the click should save the new fingerprint. They chose yes: the click saves it under the unchanged hash, through the same store write an edited chart uses, and a failed save still fails the click and saves nothing. Nothing on screen changes.

**D97 (the Path Report's empty message), 2026-10-07.** With the Analyze button gone (D87 item 6), the app's empty-report sentence still said "There are no analyzed songs to put in a report yet. Analyze some songs first." The first question about it gave a wrong premise: it described the message as the one for an empty library, and the answer it got ("There are no songs in the library yet. Click Scan library first.") would have been false wherever the message can show. The question was asked again with the real rule. The app builds this report only after a batch that was not stopped, and the batch cannot start on an empty library. It shows this sentence when the report has no rows and the database holds no results. A failed chart stores no result, so in practice every chart in the batch failed. The user chose: "None of the songs in this run could be analyzed, so there is no report to show." The same change makes the app show the report's own reason whenever the report gives one, as hydra_report already did. Before it, results with no chart library got the app's sentence in the app and the missing-library sentence in hydra_report.

**D98 (three follow-ups from the memory fixes), 2026-10-07.** Building the memory audit's fixes raised three questions. The user took the recommended answer to each:
1. **The headless UI test runner flushes the GPU every frame.** It drew frames without waiting for the software GPU, so a long `wait` piled up about 1.3 GB. Flushing every frame peaked at 191 MB and needs no new number. Waiting every frame (about 120 MB) slowed the scripts 2 to 4 times on a busy machine, and waiting every 4 frames would have added a number. The scripts are timed on a quiet machine at the join; a slowdown comes back to the user. Hydra.exe is unchanged, since it waits for vsync.
2. **A click reads the chart's names from the database.** The library rows no longer keep each chart's title, artist, charter and signature (about 6 MB). A click reads them with one indexed read on the UI thread. During a batch that read can wait on the same database lock the library's score refresh already waits on, at worst about 0.4 s at the batch-end checkpoint.
3. **A click whose file has no chart row does nothing, silently.** That can only happen in the frame between a scan replacing the chart table and the library reloading. No message is shown.

**D99 (link-time optimization only in the installer's build, mimalloc included), 2026-10-07.** Incremental link-time optimization left stale cache files that broke builds again and again on 2026-10-07. The user chose to turn link-time optimization off in the everyday build (`build-cpp\`) and keep it, as plain `/LTCG`, only in the installer's build (the `ship` preset); `docs/development.md` gives the timings. The merge review then found that mimalloc.dll had been link-time optimized in every Release build since D88, although the D88 trial measured a DLL without it: the line meant to switch it off for mimalloc never worked. The user chose to keep it on for mimalloc in the installer's build, like every other target, rather than go back to the measured DLL.

**D100 (the library file upgrade never freezes the window), 2026-10-07.** The first start of a Hydra with D87's smaller database upgrades an older hydra.db, and it did so on the window's own thread before the first frame. On a cold disk the in-place drop took about 4.4 s, and the window sat unpainted with no message. The plan moves the open to a worker thread with a startup screen, and replaces the in-place drop with a copy of the kept tables into a fresh file that is then swapped in (ADR 0026, "The upgrade"). A prototype of the copy took 0.39 s on a cold disk. The merged code (1c99432) took 0.47 s on a cold copy of the user's 292 MB library, and the window never stopped answering for more than the probe's own 49 ms. The user took the recommended answer to every question except item 4:
1. **Copy and swap, with no lasting backup.** Hydra copies charts, results and meta into `hydra.db.upgrading` beside hydra.db, swaps the two files' names, and removes the old file. This changes D87 item 7: the upgrade is no longer in place. There is still no backup copy afterwards.
2. **A fast open shows nothing.** The window stays empty and responsive until the open has run 0.15 s, the same `kViewProgressDelaySeconds` the click's "Analyzing chart..." box waits for (D87 item 6). Only then does the startup screen appear.
3. **The upgrade's screen.** A bordered box centred in the window, as wide as the scan window (`kProgressBoxWidth`, 460.0f before scaling, the scan window's existing width moved to one constant), reads "Updating your library file for this version of Hydra", then "This happens once. Your charts and results are kept.", then "Copying your library..." over a bar that counts rows copied, like "12,345 / 38,009". The third line reads "Updating the results table..." while a 1.8.x file's results table is rebuilt, and "Finishing..." with the bar full during the swap.
4. **A time-left line, by the Song Preview's rule.** The recommendation was no time-left line; the user chose to show one. It reuses the Preview's rate clock (`ByteRateClock`), its words (`detail::time_left_text`) and its 3 s wait before the first estimate. Nothing new is invented. A fast disk finishes before the line can appear.
5. **No Cancel button.** Closing the window stops the copy at its next row, removes the new file and leaves hydra.db untouched. The next start upgrades again from the beginning.
6. **The failure message.** A failed upgrade shows today's startup message box (D72 item 1) with this sentence over the raw error, then Hydra closes: "Hydra couldn't update its library file (hydra.db) for this version. Your charts and results were not changed. Check that no other copy of Hydra or hydra_batch is running and that the disk isn't full, then start Hydra again." hydra.db is left as it was.
7. **One label for a slow ordinary open.** A file that needs no upgrade but takes over 0.15 s to open, say on a drive waking from sleep, shows "Opening your library..." with no bar, for both the open and the library read.

**D101 (one fold per score in the Paths tab's list), 2026-10-07.** The Paths tab redesign (7afb806, 2026-09-27) drew the path list from the approved mockup, which had only the Optimal, Within N and Best all-0 path headings, with the score on every button. It dropped the list's older fold per score, so tied variations and lower paths looked alike. The user chose to bring the folds back inside the redesign's headings, from a mock (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-07-path-score-folds/` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-07-path-score-folds/`)). Every score under Optimal and Within gets a fold, open by default, with the score in the mono font (gold on the optimal one) and how many paths share it ("2 paths"). The buttons inside show only the path and its timing. The all-0 path has no fold and keeps its score on its button. A fold's open state is keyed by its score and lasts until Hydra closes, so a song opened again keeps its closed folds, and another song starts with every fold open unless it shares a closed score.

**D102 (the best all-0 path allows an E1), 2026-10-07.** The user's words: "allow E1 paths. if you dont get the early fill you arent skipping anything, you still activate immediately". An E1 is an activation that passed over exactly one fill, and that fill was its early fill (inside the early-fill window). A player whose SP comes ready a little later never sees it and activates on the next fill. So an activation fits the all-0 rule when it passed over no fill, or is an E1 (`allzero_activation` in `src/core/model.h`). `Path::is_allzero` and the all-0 pass's engine both ask it. An E2 or a plain 1 still skips a real fill and stays out. The E1's early fill was already optional, so the 0 ms limit (D85) never applied to it and still doesn't. The search keeps a path that can still pass its early fill apart from one that already has, so neither knocks the other out. Nothing saved changes: the all-0 list is not part of any stored summary (D87), so no results stamp bump and nothing reads Stale. The user chose to add one sentence to the heading's hint (D85 item 3), after "(no skips).": "An early fill it lets pass (E1) counts as no skip." The User Guide says the same.

**D103 (the path report and the dmleaderboards comparison become Hydra windows), 2026-10-08.** The user asked for the path report to move from a generated HTML page into Hydra, in a new window, with the HTML version removed. After seeing the mock, they asked for the dmleaderboards comparison to work the same way. They approved the design on mock boards 1 to 5 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-path-report-window-mockup/report-window.html` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-path-report-window-mockup/report-window.html`)). The spec is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-path-report-window-design.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-path-report-window-design.md`). Each window is its own OS window, made through Dear ImGui's multi-viewports, and is owned by the main window. Both share one window frame and one table view. The fill-spawn comparison stays an HTML page, because only `hydra_fillcompare` makes it. The user took the recommended answer every time:
1. **Headers sort.** The "Sort by" dropdown and its button go. A click sorts, a second click flips, and Shift+click adds a second key.
2. **No export.** The windows only show the report.
3. **A row click selects the chart in Hydra.** If the row's mode differs from the settings bar's, the settings bar switches to it first, so the Paths tab and the row agree.
4. **The column legend goes.** Each definition shows as the header's hover text. Each page's footer sentence stays, as a dim note under the table.
5. **`hydra_report` is removed.** Its only output was the page.
6. **The path report's Charts tile becomes "Charts shown".** It counts the charts among the rows that pass the filters, matching "Paths shown". The subtitle still counts every chart.
7. **The dmleaderboards comparison gets its own window.**
8. **Picking a player closes the "Compare dmleaderboards user" box and opens the window.** The box's last step (Done, Open report, Compare another, Close) goes.
9. **There is one comparison window.** Comparing another player replaces its contents.
10. **Cancel while fetching closes the window and reopens the player list,** as Cancel in the box goes back to the list today.
11. **Refresh fetches the player's scores again and rebuilds.**
12. **A "not in library" row's click does nothing.** Its hover text says "Not in your library".
13. **"Open automatically" covers only the path report.** Its hint becomes "Open the path report as soon as it's built."
14. **A report window first opens at the main window's size and position** (2026-10-08, asked after the T1 spike). Hydra remembers its placement after that, as the spec says. Its minimum size is where the controls row and the first five columns still fit, measured from the content.
15. **Tooltips, dropdowns and popups opened from the main window stay inside it, as before viewports** (2026-10-08, asked after the T1 spike). Only the report windows get their own OS windows.
16. **Text columns sort the way the Library table does** (2026-10-08, asked after T3): fold case and accents with `fold_for_search`, then plain character order. "Song 10" sorts before "Song 2". The pages' browser order is not copied.
17. **An empty Posted date sinks to the bottom in both directions,** like every other empty value (2026-10-08, asked after T3). The old page sorted it first from A to Z.
18. **The count line's noun follows `counted`'s singular-at-1 rule,** on the total: "1 of 1 path" (2026-10-08, asked after T3). The old page always wrote the plural.
19. **While a batch locks the settings bar, a click on a report row of another mode selects nothing,** and the status line shows today's "A batch is running." (2026-10-08, asked after T4). A row of the bar's own mode still selects. The Paths tab and the row never disagree.
20. **"Open path report" shows when the library has analyzed charts, when a report is in memory, or while one builds** (2026-10-08, asked after T4), so the button never vanishes while there is a report to show.
21. **The mock's two extra sentences are drawn** (2026-10-08, asked after T5): the path report's subtitle reads "Building the report from your library..." while it builds (board 2a), and the library out-of-date line ends with "(a batch finished at HH:MM)", the batch's finish time (board 2b). The settings line has no time.
22. **A settings change marks a report out of date only when it changes a setting that report reads** (2026-10-08, asked after T4's review). The path report reads the SP cap and the lens, so a mode change (including a row click's) never marks it. The comparison reads its mode and the lens. The rule has one owner beside the two report builders.
23. **The Path column sorts by item 16's rule too, with no exception** (2026-10-08, asked after the join's before-and-after check). Plain character order puts "+" before "-", so "0 1 3 0+" now sorts above "0 1 3 0- 0", the reverse of the old page's browser order. On the `testdata/input` corpus 81 of 369 rows move with Best path only off; Song, Charter, Mode and Timing showed no change.
24. **With analysis off (a bad `hydra_rules.ini`) and no report in memory, the path window shows the toolbar's existing sentence,** "hydra_rules.ini has an error, so analysis is off until the file is fixed and Hydra is restarted.", and the error under it (2026-10-08, asked after T6). It reads them from the toolbar's owner; the button stays (item 20).
25. **The building bar reads "Analyzing n of N records"** (2026-10-08, asked after T6). It counts one per chart and mode still to analyze, the unit the subtitle already calls a record. This replaces the spec's "Analyzing n of N charts". Addendum (2026-10-11, asked after the bar ended on 37,691 while the header said 38,883 on the user's library). The two counted records differently: the header counts each chart and mode once per library copy (D76, D77), and the bar counted each chart and mode once. Now the bar counts every record the page lists, each chart and mode once per library copy, as the header does. Charts a batch already analyzed count as done from the start, so the bar can open part-way along. Item 26 is unchanged: with nothing left to analyze, the bar still shows no count. A chart that fails to analyze, or that yields no path, still moves the bar but lists no record, so the header can end lower than the bar by that chart's copies.
26. **When nothing is left to analyze (a batch's results cover every chart), the building bar moves with no count,** like the comparison's bar, under the building subtitle (2026-10-08, asked after T6). No "0 of 0".
27. **The comparison's tiles count the rows that pass the filters, and its subtitle stops counting by status** (2026-10-08, asked after the hands-on check showed "1,147 scores: 20 under optimal ... 1,096 not in your library" above tiles reading "Scores 1" and "Not in library 0" with "apex" searched). The subtitle reads "<player> — N scores". The first tile reads "Scores shown", like the path report's "Charts shown". Avg % of optimal and Points left keep describing the rows on screen. The count line ("X of Y scores") is unchanged. The user did not approve the real-library memory check (the plan's join step 4). The question put to them never said "full library run" or how long it would take, and the agent's 40-minute cap was never shown to them. The run went ahead anyway, on a copy of their hydra.db, and built the report twice. The user's words afterwards: "I did not approve a full library test and I did not approve a 40 minute test." Later the same day the user chose to act on its numbers anyway; see the D95 addendum and item 28.
28. **Closing the path report window frees its rows, and closing it mid-build cancels the build** (2026-10-08, asked after the report memory run measured about 42 MB of rows, 68,037 of them, on the user's library). Until now the window's X, Esc or Ctrl+W only hid it and the rows stayed, so reopening was instant. Now closing lets the rows go, and reopening builds the report again with the building bar (about 3 s warm, about 19 s cold on that library). A build still running when the window closes stops, as its Cancel button stops it, so nothing lands in memory with the window shut. A report built after a batch while the window is closed stays until the window is opened and closed. The user picked both answers from two options each; keeping the rows and letting the build finish were the others. The comparison window is unchanged. Two follow-up answers (2026-10-09, asked after the build showed both effects). Reopening after a close keeps the window's search, timing dropdown, "Best path only" and sort, while Refresh and a batch's newly landed report still reset them to defaults; resetting on reopen too was the other option. After a close, the batch's finished strip drops "The path report is ready." and its "Open report" button, because the rows are gone; the toolbar's "Open path report" still reopens the report. Keeping that line and button, with the button rebuilding, was the other option. The strip's "Open automatically" checkbox stays after a close, because it is a setting and the strip is the only place to change it; it still hides while the report builds or after a failed build (2026-10-09). Hiding it with the line was the other option. It also shows after a cancelled build, by the window's Cancel or by closing the window mid-build, and after a "Try again" or Refresh that ends Ready; the rule is "this batch started its report, and no report is building or failed" (2026-10-09, asked after review found a successful Try again left the box hidden). Hiding it after a cancel was the other option.

The plan's "User decisions" block, copied word for word when the plan left the repo (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-path-report-window.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-path-report-window.md`)):

**User decisions:** D103, items 1 to 13 in the spec, all made on 2026-10-08.

**D104 (Clone Hero 1.1's Note Shuffle modifier), 2026-10-09.** The design is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-note-shuffle.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-note-shuffle.md`). Each question quoted the rule as the plan states it, and the user took the recommended answer every time. The questions are copied word for word after the calls. The game's shuffle rule itself now lives on the Clone Hero reference page `docs/clone-hero/02-note-list.md`, rows NL-40 to NL-46. The plan's four open questions (tick scale, note order inside a chord, when the star base is taken, and the two freeze cases) are still being settled by step 0, so nothing is built yet.
1. **The switch.** A "Note Shuffle" checkbox beside "2x Bass", off by default, saved in `hydra.ini` as `view_noteshuffle`. With it on, analysis, the Preview, the Stars and Dynamics tabs and batches all use the shuffled chart for the current difficulty, Pro Drums and 2x Bass. Its tooltip: "Score the chart as Clone Hero 1.1's Note Shuffle modifier rearranges it. The rearrangement is the same every time for a given chart and drum setting."
2. **The mode name.** With the switch on, the mode string gains ", Note Shuffle" at the end, as in "Easy Drums, 1x Bass, Note Shuffle". With it off the string is unchanged, so every saved row keeps its key.
3. **Pro Drums off gets its own row too.** With no cymbals the shuffle should leave score, path and stars as they were, and a batch diff on `testdata/input` will check that. Hydra still stores "… Drums, … Note Shuffle" as its own row, so one rule covers every setting. Reusing the unshuffled row was the other option.
4. **One results stamp.** The feature adds keys only, so `kResultsStamp` is not bumped for it. A later fix to the shuffle alone bumps the one stamp and marks every row Stale. A second stamp for shuffled rows only was the other option.
5. **A chart the game can't shuffle gets no path.** If the game tests confirm the freeze, Hydra finds no path for that chart and setting, and shows a sentence like "Clone Hero freezes loading this chart with Note Shuffle on." the way an unreadable chart shows today. A capped redraw loop and a fall-back to the unshuffled chart were the other options.
6. **dmleaderboards Compare is greyed out while the switch is on,** with "dmleaderboards scores don't say whether Note Shuffle was on, so they can't be compared with a shuffled path." With it off nothing changes.
7. **No ship until the note order is settled.** If one code-reading pass and one game test leave the order inside a chord unknown, Note Shuffle waits. Shipping with a tooltip warning was the other option.

The plan's "Questions for you", copied word for word when the plan left the repo. Its heading is set in bold so it stays under this record:

**Questions for you**

Each one quotes the rule as the code would apply it.

**Q1. The switch.** Rule: a checkbox labelled "Note Shuffle" beside "2x Bass", off by default, saved as `view_noteshuffle`. When it's on, every analysis, the Preview, the Stars and Dynamics tabs, and batches use the shuffled chart for the current difficulty, Pro Drums and 2x Bass. Proposed tooltip: "Score the chart as Clone Hero 1.1's Note Shuffle modifier rearranges it. The rearrangement is the same every time for a given chart and drum setting." Do you want this label and tooltip, or different words?

**Q2. The mode name.** Rule: when the switch is on, the mode string gains ", Note Shuffle" at the end. When it's off, it doesn't change. At one end, "Expert Pro Drums, 2x Bass" stays exactly that, and every saved row keeps working. At the other, "Easy Drums, 1x Bass, Note Shuffle" is a new key. This string is what the path report's Mode column, the batch confirm line and the dmleaderboards subtitle show. Is this name right?

**Q3. Pro Drums off.** With Pro Drums off there are no cymbals, so the shuffle only changes which tom each note lands on. The score, path and stars should come out identical to the unshuffled chart; only the Preview looks different. A batch diff on `testdata/input` will prove it. Rule I recommend: still store a separate row for "… Drums, … Note Shuffle", with one rule for every setting and no special case. The cost is one extra analysis per chart and setting. The alternative is to reuse the unshuffled row and shuffle only the Preview. Which do you want?

**Q4. A separate stamp for shuffled rows?** The results stamp rule today: bump `kResultsStamp` "whenever analysis output changes in a way the hydra_rules.ini fingerprint doesn't already catch", and every saved row reads Stale. This feature adds new keys only, so it needs no bump. Later, though, a fix to the shuffle alone, say after a game test, would mark every row Stale under that rule, unshuffled ones too. Rows re-analyze on click, so that's cheap. My recommendation is to keep the one stamp. The alternative is a second stamp in `stored_versions.h`, written only on shuffled rows, so a shuffle fix marks only those Stale. Keep one stamp, or add a second?

**Q5. Charts the game freezes on.** This only applies if Open 4's game tests show a real freeze. Rule: when the shuffle can't finish for a chart and setting, Hydra finds no path, and the library and details show a sentence like "Clone Hero freezes loading this chart with Note Shuffle on." It's shown the way an unreadable chart is today. Under Pro Drums off, a four-colour chord (red, yellow, blue and green toms together) followed by a different chord hits it. Under Pro Drums on, red + yellow cymbal + blue tom + green cymbal followed by a lone snare hits it. A normal two-pad groove never does. I don't recommend the other options: carrying on with a capped loop invents a chart nobody can play, and falling back to the unshuffled chart is silently wrong. Is the sentence right, and is "no path" the right outcome?

**Q6. dmleaderboards.** Rule: while Note Shuffle is on, "Compare with dmleaderboards…" is greyed out with "dmleaderboards scores don't say whether Note Shuffle was on, so they can't be compared with a shuffled path." With the switch off, nothing changes. Is that the behaviour and wording you want?

**Q7. If the order can't be settled.** If Open 2 stays unsettled after one reading pass and one game test, I recommend not shipping Note Shuffle until it is, because some charts would shuffle wrongly without anything saying so. The other choice is to ship with a warning in the tooltip. Which?

**D105 (a 2x kick and a normal kick on one tick merge, as in Clone Hero), 2026-10-09.** Note Shuffle's step 0 read how the game handles the two on the same tick (`docs/audit/note-shuffle/step0/FINDINGS.md`, Open 2). Both of the game's readers keep them as two notes. A clean-up step then merges them into one kick that carries the 2x mark and any ghost or accent marks. With 2x Bass off, the game removes every 2x kick, so that tick has no kick. Hydra with 2x Bass off never reads the 2x kick ([song.cpp:836](../../src/parse/song.cpp), [song.cpp:1509](../../src/parse/song.cpp)), so it keeps the normal kick in either file order and scores a kick the game doesn't have. With 2x Bass on, both have one kick, but Hydra marks it 2x only when the 2x kick comes first in the file. The first question put to the user wrongly said Hydra already matched when the 2x kick came first. It was asked again with the rule above, and the answer didn't change. The user chose to match the game after a game test (songs F1 and F2 in `docs/audit/note-shuffle/game-tests/`). If the game shows no kick on those ticks with 2x Bass off, Hydra merges the two and the results stamp is bumped, so every saved row reads Stale. If the game shows a kick, nothing changes. None of the 55 charts with 2x kicks in `testdata/input` puts one on a normal kick's tick. Leaving Hydra as it is and changing it without a game test were the other options. This closes the open question in the memory note `derivation-audit-2026-10-03` once the test is run.

**D106 (agent rules: approval first, time limits, the code as the source of truth, and fresh agents for review fixes), 2026-10-09.** The user added these to the agent rules in `CLAUDE.md` and answered the follow-up questions.
1. **Nothing starts without the user's yes.** Every plan and every build waits for it. A build is any piece of work: writing code, editing hooks, or launching agents to do either. A code-only change needs no approval of its design, but its build still waits for the yes (rule 3).
2. **Time limits.** An agent starts wrapping up at 20 minutes of real time and stops at 30. The tool-call limits stay, and whichever comes first applies (rule 7). The progress hook enforces it, counting from the agent's first tool call.
3. **The code is the source of truth.** Never trust another agent's summary or the comments it left behind; check claims against the code and the source data (rule 9). The user asked for no hook.
4. **Models.** Read-only scouts run on Sonnet (rule 2). No work goes to Haiku; that lives in the posture file and the dispatch hooks' messages, not in `CLAUDE.md`.
5. **Review fixes go to fresh agents.** (Replaced by D107.) The progress hook counts an agent's 30 minutes from its first call and never restarts the count, so an agent woken after a long review is stopped at once, and D61's exchange changes. The reviewer reports its findings and ends. A fresh agent fixes them once, and a second fresh agent checks the fixes, fixes anything left and signs off. When the main session wrote a document, it makes the fixes itself; the reviewer and the checker are still fresh agents. Restarting an agent's clock after a long gap was the other option. D61's one round and its rules for leftovers stand. The briefs are `docs/agents/derive-once-review.md`, `doc-review.md`, `fix-round.md` and `brief-preamble.md`.

**D107 (one reviewer fixes its own findings, then done), 2026-10-10.** The user replaced D61's exchange and D106 item 5. The .sng and .srb song id fix (ADR 0028) was about 200 lines of code, and its review took six fresh agents, about 781k subagent tokens and two refused merges (counted in the session handoff `docs/handoffs/2026-10-10-review-pipeline-efficiency-handoff.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-10-review-pipeline-efficiency-handoff.md`), which was still uncommitted in the main checkout when this was written). The user called this "a massive waste of time" and set the rule: "one reviewer who fixes his findings directly, then we're done. no more follow ups."
1. **One review per change.** One fresh agent reviews the change's code and documents, fixes every finding itself, commits its fixes and signs off. Then the change merges. There is no separate fixer, no checker and no second round (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5).
2. **Nothing goes back to the user.** A finding whose fix needs the user (a new number, a change to what is shown or stored) goes in the reviewer's Notes. The main session passes it on, and the merge does not wait for it.
3. **The gates.** The derive-once gate already accepts a reviewer that wrote some of the commits but not all of them. The doc-review submit gate now accepts a reviewer that commits its own fix to a document it did not write. The reviewer's tool-call cap rises from 80 to the 150 every agent has, because it now fixes too. The workflow-script hook no longer demands a follow-up agent after a review.
4. **The briefs.** `docs/agents/derive-once-review.md` and `docs/agents/doc-review.md` say how the reviewer fixes. `docs/agents/fix-round.md` (removed; `git show 4773cb21:docs/agents/fix-round.md`) is retired.
5. **Notes and running out (the user's answers, same day).** The reviewer's own review asked two questions. Notes that reach the user without holding the merge are fine: "notes are fine". And a reviewer that runs out of time or tool calls signs off what it fixed and lists the rest in its Notes; nothing is handed to a fresh agent. The user: "yes do that for running out".

**D108 (how long a test may wait), 2026-10-10.** A test that waits for a background job with no limit freezes the whole run when the job hangs, until CI's own limit kills everything. The user chose two caps, and `tests/wait_util.h` is their one home.
1. **One wait inside a test process stops at 30 seconds** (`testwait::kWaitCap`). The shared waits are `testwait::wait_until` and `testwait::tick_until_thrown`; the GUI harness keeps its own `wait_until` (item 3). A shared wait that runs past its cap fails the test, and the failure names what it waited for.
2. **One `hydra_uitest --jobs` child process stops at 5 minutes** (`testwait::kUitestProcessCap`). The runner stops a child that runs past it and fails that test by name, with "the test process ran past 300 s and was stopped".
3. **GUI waits longer than the process cap are accepted as they are** (the user's answer to the reviewer's question, same day). The GUI harness's own waits allow up to 300 s, for example the wait for a stopped batch to finish in `tests/ui/uitest_batch_reports.cpp`. A test that hung there would hit the 5-minute process cap first, so its own wait never reports. That is fine, because the runner still fails that test by name.

**D109 (a sound device that won't start), 2026-10-10.** Before this, the Preview's sound device could open and then fail to start, and Hydra said nothing: Play moved the highway with no sound and no hint why. A device that would not open already showed a warning. The user chose to show both failures the same way.
1. **A start failure takes the open failure's path.** The chart still loads and plays muted, and the same warning line shows above the highway. It clears when the next song loads. Play stays usable.
2. **One line for both failures:** "Hydra couldn't use your audio device; the preview is muted." It replaces "No audio device found; the preview is muted." Hovering it still shows which step failed.

**D110 (columns sized by usual width, then grown), 2026-10-10.** The one column-width rule shared a table's room in proportion to each column's widest cell. On the user's library of 19,499 charts, one chart's Best path text is 1,128 characters long, while 99% are under 40. That one row took nearly all the room, and Title and Artist were cut with most of the window blank (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-10-column-widths-ui-regression-handoff.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-10-column-widths-ui-regression-handoff.md`)). The user chose "usual width, then grow" over an outlier cut like MUI's and a per-column cap.
1. **A column's usual width fits 99 of every 100 rows** (`kUsualShare` in `src/ui/column_widths.cpp`, its one home). It is the 99th-percentile cell width, nearest rank, and never less than the header. With fewer than 100 rows it is the widest cell.
2. **Spare room goes to the columns still cut, the one closest to fitting first.** When the room covers every usual width, each cut column gets its usual width and the rest grows them toward their widest, smallest gap first. When it doesn't, they share it in proportion to their usual widths, down to their headers. An outlier row ends in `…` and shows its full text on hover.

**D111 (the Preview's notation line and star counter), 2026-10-10.** The user asked for the next activation's notation and a star counter in the Preview, and picked from a clickable mock (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-10-preview-next-act-stars-mock/preview_mock.html` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-10-preview-next-act-stars-mock/preview_mock.html`)): option B for the notation and option 2 for the stars. The mock's drawing values are the user's approval of the look.
1. **The notation gets its own line** in the Next activation box, between the header and the "at …" line, at 17/12 of the body text size (`kNextActNotationScale`, the mock's 17 px over 12 px). Its text is `Activation::notationstr_verbose`, which Copy path's path string (`Path::pathstring_verbose`) also uses for each activation.
2. **Seven stars under the score**, earned ones in the Star Power gold and the rest grey outlines, then a grey "star N in X" line that goes away at 7 stars. The star shape is the mock's: inner radius 0.47 of the outer, outline stroke 0.07 of the star's height, outline grey 138. Stars count the score without the solo bonus (`src/core/stars.h`).
3. **The Next activation box moved to the top right, under the drain box** (the user's call, same day). At the bottom left the highway is widest, so a long notation line either wrapped or shrank every box. The highway is narrowest at the top, so the box has room there. Its right edge lines up with the drain box's right edge. The gap above it is the same gap the left column keeps between the time box and the score box. Its text stays left-aligned, in the same colours and line order. With no SP gauge, the drain box is hidden and this box takes its place. The header and the "at …" line may still wrap, to the room beside the highway at the box's lowest row; the notation line never wraps (`render::wrap_right_box`).

**D112 (drum lanes in the Preview), 2026-10-10.** The user asked for the Preview to show drum lanes: the strips Clone Hero draws down one or two pad columns during a roll or trill. A scout read how the game builds them in Clone Hero 1.1.0.6142 (its listings are in `docs/audit/drum-lanes/`), and the main session checked the .mid facts against `testdata`. Onyx and the game disagree on which pads light; in `testdata`, 4 of 50 lanes differ, all single lanes that start on a chord (for example *Victoria – Grow* at tick 29856, a red roll that opens on a blue crash). The user chose:
1. **Onyx's look.** Each lit pad gets Onyx's lane strip at full strength, drawn before the fill strips, as Onyx's `drawLanes` does (ADR 0008).
2. **The game's rules for what a lane is.** A .chart lane is special phrase `S 65` (single) or `S 66` (double) in a difficulty's section, and shows on that difficulty. A .mid lane is pitch 126 (single lane marker) or pitch 127 (double lane marker) on the drum track, note-on to note-off. Its note-on velocity names the lowest difficulty that shows it, and every higher difficulty shows it too: velocity 21 to 30 is Easy, velocity 31 to 40 is Medium, velocity 41 to 50 is Hard, and any other velocity is Expert.
3. **The game's rule for which pads light.** Kicks never count. The first chord with a non-kick note, from one tick before the lane to its end, decides. A single lane lights that chord's lowest pad in the game's order: red, yellow tom, blue tom, green tom, yellow cymbal, blue cymbal, green cymbal. A double lane adds the highest pad of a second chord: the first chord itself when it has two or more pads, otherwise the next chord with a non-kick note. A lane with no such chord is dropped. The pads are picked before Note Shuffle runs, as in the game. The rule's one home is `light_drum_lanes` in `src/parse/song.cpp`.
4. **Any note inside the lane counts.** The game drops a lane whose first note lands more than a small tolerance after its start. That tolerance is resolution × 12 divided by a value the game sets at runtime, and nobody has read that value. The user chose not to guess it. All 50 lanes in `testdata` start exactly on their first note.

The game also reads lanes during play, to make the notes under a lane easier to hit. Hydra's engine does not, and this decision changes no score.

*Correction, 2026-10-17.* Item 3's sentence "The pads are picked before Note Shuffle runs, as in the game" was wrong. Hydra now picks a lane's pads after Note Shuffle, from the shuffled notes. The rule for which chords decide is unchanged. Kicks never move and the shuffle removes no note, so the same chords decide either way; only their colours change.

The user found it in the Preview. In *Glide* by Between the Buried and Me, around 1:51, a red lane strip sat over yellow cymbals and blue toms with Note Shuffle on.

The listings show why. The game does build its lanes before its shuffle: the chart builder in `docs/audit/note-shuffle/step0/open2/f20d2ea0_build.asm` calls the lane builder at 0x20D317E and the shuffle at 0x20D3315. But a lane does not store a colour. The lane builder in `docs/audit/drum-lanes/f20d12c0.asm` keeps a reference to the lane's first chord (saved to rsp+0x70 at 0x20D1585) and, for a double lane, to the second chord (saved to rsp+0x38 at 0x20D15FB; a single lane stores null there at 0x20D1625). It writes those references into the lane at +0x20 (0x20D17AA) and +0x28 (0x20D17BC). It reads each chord's lane mask, the 16-bit word at +0x80, through a note it reaches from that chord (0x20D15BA and 0x20D1619). The shuffle in `docs/audit/note-shuffle/step0/open2/f20f0c30_shuffle.asm` rewrites that same word in place on the notes it walks (0x20F1151). So the lane holds notes, and the shuffle recolours them after the lane is built.

The routine that draws a lane was not read. The claim that the drawn strip follows the recoloured notes rests on the fields above and on the user's Glide report. The rule's one home is still `light_drum_lanes`, now called once from the loader tail after the shuffle switch (`finish_loaded_song` in `src/parse/song.cpp`). D254 later changed which pads a lane lights while Note Shuffle is on; with it off, item 3 stands.

**D113 (the concept duplication audit), 2026-10-11.** The derive-once reviews catch a rule written twice line by line, but not a module that builds its own version of an idea under its own names. The user asked for an audit of those module-level copies and approved its plan, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-concept-duplication-audit.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-concept-duplication-audit.md`), with "go with recommendations". The plan's "User decisions" section holds the reasons, and it is copied word for word after the decisions; the decisions are:
1. **Scope.** All of `src/`, no pilot. `tools/` and `tests/` wait.
2. **Two scout waves.** Ten agents first (eight sibling groups and the two inventory scouts), then the other eight groups, fed the first wave's candidates.
3. **The clue script.** It is saved beside the audit as a record, and its probes become a new check 5 in `tools/derive_once_precheck.ps1`. No second scanner goes under `tools/`.
4. **Copies that agree today get fixed.** Every K1 finding in `src/` is fixed, in waves the user can stop between.
5. **Decisions go here.** The audit's user decisions continue the D-numbers in this file.
6. **Kind F.** The derive-once review gains a sixth kind letter, F, for a second home of an idea that already has one, and a step 8 that checks every new type, enum, status word set and helper family against `CONTEXT.md`.
7. **Status words first.** The user-visible status words of the library, the DM page and the fill page are decided as one batch of questions before the fix wave, so the fix wave is code-only.
8. **The clue thresholds.** A repeated string counts at 12 or more characters. A struct counts at 3 or more fields, and two structs match at 4 or more shared field names. On 2026-10-11 these gave 48 clues over `src/` (the plan's phase 1 counts: 17 strings, 9 names, 17 struct pairs and 5 store methods). The first real run of check 5 may tune them, as a reviewed change.

The plan's "User decisions" block, copied word for word when the plan left the repo (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-concept-duplication-audit.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-concept-duplication-audit.md`)). Its heading is set in bold so it stays under this record:

**User decisions needed before this starts**

**Approved 2026-10-11.** The user read a summary of this plan and replied "go with recommendations". Every recommendation below is the decision, recorded as D113 in `docs/audit/2026-10-03-fix-decisions.md`.

1. **Scope.** `src/` only, all eleven folders, no pilot. Recommendation: yes, as asked. `tools/` and `tests/` wait.

2. **Scout waves.** Sixteen group scouts and two inventory scouts as two waves (ten, then eight fed the first wave's candidates), or one wave of eighteen. Recommendation: two waves, for the dedupe; the cost is about twenty minutes of wall time.

3. **Where the lead script lives.** Saved beside the audit as a record (`leads.ps1`), with its probes folded into the precheck as check 5 in phase 7; or added under `tools/` now as its own script. Recommendation: the record plus check 5, so there is one precheck and not a second scanner.

4. **Fix the K1 findings that agree today, or only record them.** The line-level audit's "duplicates that agree today" were its largest group (166 of 352), and each is a future split. Recommendation: fix every K1 in `src/`, in waves the user can stop between.

5. **Where decisions are written.** Continue the D-numbers in `docs/audit/2026-10-03-fix-decisions.md`, or start a file in the audit folder. Recommendation: continue, because the precheck reads `docs/audit/*decisions*.md` and the file is where D107 and D112 already are.

6. **Kind F.** Add a sixth kind letter to the review page and the precheck, or fold module-level homes into kind A. Recommendation: kind F, so a reviewer's finding says which of the two it is and the precheck's line can be told from a row hit.

7. **K2 words up front.** Decide the user-visible status words (the library's, the DM page's, the fill page's) as one batch of questions before the fix wave, so the fix wave is code-only. Recommendation: yes; the questions are written from the verified predicates in phase 5.

8. **The probe thresholds.** A string of 12 or more characters, a struct with 3 or more fields, 4 or more shared field names. These shaped this session's lead counts and would shape check 5. Recommendation: keep them, recorded as a D-record, with the counts above as the reason; the first run of check 5 on a real range may tune them, as a reviewed change.

**D114 (the concept audit's answers and the fix wave), 2026-10-11.** The user read the 21 questions in `docs/audit/2026-10-11-concept-audit/questions.md` and replied "go with recommended". Every question's recommendation is the decision, and the reply is the yes to the fix wave (D113 item 4: in waves the user can stop between). Each item names its question and finding; the question holds the predicates, examples and the case against.
1. **Q1 (M01).** The comparison page's "not analyzed" becomes "no current result", its own definition in `dm_report.h`. The bucket and its count stay whole; a Stale chart is counted there as today.
2. **Q2 (M02).** One spelling, "your library", wherever the comparison page writes "not in library" as a sentence, with the bare table cell kept lower case. The fill page's tile and subtitle for "in both" read "In both, not both scored".
3. **Q3 (M03).** The fill page sorts text the way the windows do: each row carries a folded sort field from C++ and the script compares bytes. The Library sorts on the text it shows, not the stored text.
4. **Q4 (M05).** The fill page's count line takes a singular and plural noun, so one chart reads "1 of 1 chart" (D103 item 18 on every page).
5. **Q5 (M07).** The fill page stays HTML. C++ injects every word, noun and colour the script writes; only the loops stay in the script.
6. **Q6 (M17).** `collect_dm_rows` reads the difficulty back from the parsed chart mode, so Refresh, Try again and "Compare another player..." refuse a non-Expert comparison with the toolbar's sentence, shown as a failed build.
7. **Q7 (M29).** After a rescan, the open song panel re-reads its chart's entry by `row_key`, the lookup `select` uses, so the panel follows the library row.
8. **Q8 (M73).** The rules file reads numbers through `parse_finite_number`; `nan`, `inf` and hex are reported with the rules file's usual error, for decimal and whole-number keys alike.
9. **Q9 (M15).** `hydra_batch`'s header uses the app's names and number forms for each setting ("Score range", "Path limit", "off"). The count-with-noun mapping and the "bar"/"bars" noun get one owner each.
10. **Q10 (M24).** One function words the batch's three counts, in the app's order and gloss ("skipped (already had a result)"), and the app and `hydra_batch` both print it.
11. **Q11 (M72).** The command-line tools print "hydra_rules.ini has an error:" and then the loader's line. The dead `kRulesFile` text is deleted, and the app's analysis-off sentence moves into `user_messages.cpp`.
12. **Q12 (M81).** "A batch is running." is the one sentence for that refusal, in the status line and the greyed buttons' tooltip.
13. **Q13 (M82).** The song panel shows the toolbar's analysis-off sentence, without the file's error (the toolbar above already shows it).
14. **Q14 (M83).** The status line keeps its short one-line form. The comparison window's failure sentence becomes "The comparison could not be built.", matching the path report's shape. The three identical path-report literals become one constant.
15. **Q15 (M23).** Every chart name field is trimmed once in `discover_charts`, before the unknown-name fallbacks, whatever the file format. The chart-metadata stamp is bumped so every chart's metadata is re-read on the next scan (one rule for stored versions: `src/store/stored_versions.h`, ADR 0018).
16. **Q16 (M31).** The Paths tab's "Notes:" label stays as it is.
17. **Q17 (M47).** The Preview's scrubber spans no further than playback can reach (the shorter of the song length and the playback end). The scrubber, the thumb and the shown time read one range value from the transport. The gold marks stay placed by the song length.
18. **Q18 (M50).** The Preview's end labels stay as they are; the User Guide's explanation of the clock stands (D56 item 3).
19. **Q19 (M66).** Mapped audio files keep the stricter share mode, and a comment states the reason at the open.
20. **Q20 (M74).** Writing a file through a temporary file and a rename becomes one helper, and the settings file is saved through it.
21. **Q21 (M77).** Both landing orders stay: a finished report keeps its result after a late Cancel, and a click's analysis is dropped. One shared outcome function holds the rule, with the click's exception written beside it.
22. **The code-only findings.** Every finding the questions file lists as code-only is fixed with the owner its verifier proposed; none was vetoed. That includes M52's visible side effect: the Preview's jump buttons grey during a load, where today they are live and do nothing.
23. **The fill label in `hydra_batch`'s header (asked 2026-10-11, after item 9).** The header labels the fill setting "Fills", the batch confirm's label, not the panel's checkbox name "1.0 fills". The panel keeps its checkbox name.
24. **Whole numbers in hydra_rules.ini (asked 2026-10-11, after item 8).** A whole-number key accepts any finite number that is whole, so "4.0" and "1e1" load as 4 and 10. A real fraction ("2.5"), `nan`, `inf` and hex are refused. One number grammar for the whole file.
25. **The scrubber sentence in the User Guide (asked 2026-10-11, after F5's review).** The Preview paragraph gains: "When the song's stated length runs past the last note and the audio, the scrubber ends where playback ends." The gold marks still sit by the song's length. It goes through the public doc review.
26. **`hydra_fillcompare`'s summary line (asked 2026-10-11, after F2's review).** The count of charts in both databases that are not scored on both sides reads "in both, not both scored", the page's words from item 2, in place of "with a score on one side only".
27. **One helper draws every Preview box (asked 2026-10-11, after the F5 screenshots).** The time, score, SP drain and next-activation boxes draw their panel and text through one shared helper, and measure their size through the same rule. Text is left-aligned in every box (the drain box was the only right-aligned one). The text inset is the same on all four sides of every box: the preview config's time-box margin (10 px in the shipped file, scaled like the rest), in place of today's mix (time and score 10 left, 16 right; drain and next 8 all round). Panel colour, corner rounding by which edge a box sits against, line colours and sizes, and where each box sits stay as they are.
28. **The settings file keeps CRLF line ends (asked 2026-10-11, after G5's review).** G5's `write_file_replacing` writes in binary mode, so `hydra_settings.ini` would come out LF-only after its next save. The file keeps Windows CRLF line ends, byte for byte the shape it has today; the shared writer writes text files with CRLF.

**D115 (one record per chart and profile), 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-15.** The path report was the one slow thing Hydra did: on the user's library it ran 37,691 analyses to show its rows (`docs/handoffs/2026-10-11-path-report-speed-handoff.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-11-path-report-speed-handoff.md`)), and the word "record" had four meanings. The user asked for one record per chart, and for every question about charts, records, optimal paths, squeezes, scoring and chart timing to be answered in one place. The design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-one-record-per-chart-and-profile-design.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-one-record-per-chart-and-profile-design.md`), the build plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-one-record-per-chart.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-one-record-per-chart.md`) with its part files, and the store's decision record is ADR 0031 (`docs/adr/0031-the-store-keeps-one-record-per-chart-and-profile.md`), which supersedes ADR 0026 in part. The fill and timing rules are ADR 0010's note of 2026-10-15. The user's own words for items 1 to 5 are D211 to D241 below. Those entries carry UTC dates, so a ruling made on the evening of 2026-10-11 local time sits under 2026-10-12 there; the dates in this entry are the local ones. Items 6 to 8 have no D-number yet. The questions behind item 6 were in `docs/handoffs/2026-10-14-d115-build-continue-handoff.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-14-d115-build-continue-handoff.md`) and the ones behind item 7 in `docs/handoffs/2026-10-14-d115-build-continue-2-handoff.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/handoffs/2026-10-14-d115-build-continue-2-handoff.md`); both are copied word for word after the list. The user's answers to item 7's question and to item 8 are first written down here, from the user's chat. What was decided:
1. **The store (2026-10-11).** One record per chart and profile, the "lean profiles" layout, with paths in their own table, not JSON. No limit on profiles. A player sees "charts", never "records".
2. **The report's words (2026-10-11).** An open report shows the out-of-date strip when a save changes the library. The chip reads "No timing needed", the checkbox "Optimal paths only", the count columns "Note count", and "Average Multiplier" is the one label everywhere, the Stars tab included. The Path limit tooltip is reworded to say what the search does.
3. **The Analyze button (2026-10-11).** It follows the status chip and reads "Analyze shown (N)..." when a search or a chip narrows the table.
4. **Clone Hero's timing rules (2026-10-11).** Generated fills start on the measure line, with Clone Hero's +1 tick and first candidate. One measure grid, Clone Hero's, serves measure lines, fills and the position text. Star Power's length follows Clone Hero's continuous measure count. Beat lines follow Clone Hero's grid, and the position text's beat number counts the same beats. Section names read as Clone Hero shows them, from its list, which Hydra ships. The time signature label and the position share one owner. `hydra_replay`'s dump prints positions as the screen does.
5. **The plan's ten questions (2026-10-12, each as recommended).** The upgrade keeps old rows as Stale records, and the report's fill pass goes. The report's Mode column and the row click's mode switch go. The empty database reads "Nothing is analyzed yet. Click a song or run a batch to analyze it." A click marks both open reports out of date. Both tooltips take the plan's words. A generated fill copies Clone Hero, so its length may be 0 or less. `plusmeasure` walks in exact whole numbers. Weak beats are drawn as ordinary beat lines. The results stamp becomes "2.8.0". The dump prints only the label and the decimal.
6. **Old library files and the fill rule (2026-10-14).** Files older than 2.2.0 are cut off: the app sets hydra.db aside as hydra.db.pre-2.2.0, replacing an earlier one, starts a fresh library and rescans, and the command-line tools refuse such a file and never rename it. Clone Hero 1.0 mode keeps its old fill generator exactly. The file-wide fill-rule label is retired, one file holds both rules, and `hydra_batch` may write `--legacy-fills` results into the app's own hydra.db. Star Power's length uses the exact measure count in both modes. A time signature whose beat is under one tick is refused with the existing "time signature at tick N" sentence. The batch's save-failure detail stays "saving a record failed:".
7. **The empty-report sentence (2026-10-14 and 2026-10-15).** Under settings with no records, the path report names the chart mode first, and "2x Bass" stays in it, because the mode has one spelling: "Nothing is analyzed under these settings (Expert Pro Drums, 2x Bass, SP cap 8, Clone Hero 1.1 fills). Analyze with these settings, or change them."
8. **The fill comparison (2026-10-15).** It reads one `--db`, and a chart with a record under one rule only still gets a row. Its page calls those rows "Only under 1.0", "Only under 1.1" and "Under one rule only".

From the first handoff, behind item 6, copied word for word when the handoff left the repo. Its heading is set in bold so it stays under this record:

**User decisions made 2026-10-14**

These come on top of the ten answers in the plan.

1. A time signature whose beat is under one tick is refused with Hydra's existing "time signature at tick N" sentence. That now reads "bottom number that is out of range" for "0 = TS 1 12" at resolution 192.
2. The batch's save-failure detail stays "saving a record failed:".
3. Files older than 2.2.0 are cut off. The app renames hydra.db to hydra.db.pre-2.2.0, replacing an earlier backup, starts a fresh library and rescans. The notice reads: "This library file is from a Hydra version older than 2.2.0, so Hydra set it aside as hydra.db.pre-2.2.0 and started a fresh library." If the rename fails, the error reads: "This library file is from a Hydra version older than 2.2.0, and Hydra could not set it aside: <reason>. Close any program using hydra.db and start Hydra again."
4. hydra_batch and hydra_fillcompare refuse a pre-2.2.0 file and never rename it. The sentence (proposed by the executor, matching the approved description) is: "The library file '<path>' is from a Hydra version older than 2.2.0, and opening it in Hydra sets it aside and starts a fresh library."
5. Clone Hero 1.0 mode keeps its old fill generator exactly: the old chord choice and the half-measure length.
6. The file-level fill-rule label is retired; one file holds both rules.
7. hydra_batch's refusal of `--legacy-fills` into hydra.db is dropped.
8. Star Power length uses the exact whole-number measure count in both modes, 1.0 included. "Keep 1.0 exactly as before" is about fills only.

From the second handoff, behind item 7, copied word for word when the handoff left the repo. Its headings are set in bold so they stay under this record:

**User decisions made this session**

1. The empty-under-settings sentence names the chart mode first ("yes go with recommend", 2026-10-14). The reviewer built it from the one mode spelling, `app::chartmode_key`. The path report now reads: "Nothing is analyzed under these settings (Expert Pro Drums, 2x Bass, SP cap 8, Clone Hero 1.1 fills). Analyze with these settings, or change them."

**Open questions for the user**

Ask these at the start of the next session. Neither blocks A1-5.

1. The sentence above names "2x Bass", which the user's example ("Expert Pro Drums, SP cap 4, ...") did not. `chartmode_key` always names the bass setting, so dropping it needs a second spelling of the mode. Keep it, or drop it?
2. The fill comparison page shares `nothing_under_settings`, so its empty sentence changed too. It now puts the mode first: "Nothing is analyzed under these settings (Expert Pro Drums, 2x Bass, SP cap 4) in either database. Analyze with these settings, or change them." Is that fine?
3. A2-4's reviewer noticed one small difference on the fill comparison page. When a chart has a record only in the 1.0 database, the page takes its name from the 1.0 library, as it did before this work. The main session's brief had said "names from the 1.1 library first". It was left as before. The main session told the user, and they have not answered yet.
