**D249 (session 048e660f), 2026-10-15. The path report covers the whole library.** The path report listed only the charts with a Ready record under the current settings and said nothing about the rest: 19,356 charts shown of 19,512 library rows on the user's library (the main session's count on a copy of the user's db). The user asked "when did you decide it was acceptable to not include everything?" The user had never agreed to leaving charts out. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-report-covers-whole-library.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-report-covers-whole-library.md`), and the user answered its eight questions in chat:
1. **Which failures are remembered.** Every failure the chart's own content causes (option b): NoNotes, ChartTimingRefused, NoteShuffleFreezes and ChartUnreadable. Failures with another cause (a missing file, a failed hash, running out of memory, a failed save, a bug in the search) are not remembered, so a later batch tries them again.
2. **How the failure is stored.** Two nullable columns on `records` and a fourth status, CantAnalyze (option a). The results stamp does not move.
3. **The library row.** The Best path cell reads "Can't analyze", dim, with the reason in the hover (option a). A click still analyzes the chart.
4. **The chip.** A fifth chip, "Can't analyze (N)" (option a).
5. **The coverage line.** The line names the reason when every chart shares one ("59 charts have no Expert Pro Drums notes", the user's own wording), otherwise "N charts can't be analyzed", and Show list groups the charts by reason (option a).
6. **The batch.** A batch skips these charts as "already had a result", the confirm adds the dim line "N of these can't be analyzed. Check the box to try them again.", and the redo box tries them again (option a).
7. **The command-line tool.** Nothing more (the recommendation; the main session took it).
8. **The progress while the report analyzes.** Opening the report runs an ordinary batch, with the strip, and the window shows "Analyzing n of N charts" over a real bar (the recommendation). Added by the user: when more than 500 charts need analyzing, the report shows the normal batch confirm first ("Analyze N charts that have no result yet?"); at 500 or fewer it just runs. The 500 is the number the question offered and the user chose.

**D250 (session c35a4b7d), 2026-10-15. Early fills: orange timing up to 85 ms.** The user asked to "change the early fill search to allow orange timing up to 85ms". Orange is the side of the early-fill window where Star Power comes ready after the fill's deadline, so you must hit early to summon the fill; white is the side where it comes ready before the deadline with time to spare. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-early-fill-orange-85.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-early-fill-orange-85.md`). The user's calls in chat:
1. **Which side.** Only the orange side goes to 85 ms (`fill_refuses`). The white side, where an activation gets the `E` mark with nothing to time (`is_e0`), stays at 60 ms, so D51 call 7's clutter does not come back.
2. **Fixed or setting.** A fixed 85 ms constant of its own, not the hit window setting, which stays display-only.
3. **Saved results.** Every analyzed chart reads Stale: the results stamp becomes "2.8.0+efill85".
4. **Comments.** Added by the user: "comments exist to explain the current code, not give a history lesson". The new constants' comments say what each means and who reads it, with no old values or versions.
5. **What it changes, and the notation.** The user said 1.1 fills gain nothing, as the 1.7.9 check showed, and asked for a library run under 1.0 fills. That run used the user's settings (depth 4, 10 ms path limit, SP cap 4) on fresh scratch databases, with 18,858 charts getting paths. 25 best paths score higher (+80 to +10,040), each with a required early fill between 60.1 and 81.9 ms, and none score lower. 48 more keep their score, but their notation gains a passed-over E fill (Aerosmith - Cryin' goes from `2 1 9 0` to `2 E2 9 0`). Asked whether to keep those, the user said: "Keep them. The extra E fills in the notation are fine."

**D251 (session 5b35957b), 2026-10-16. Preview: a solo box, and a 10 ms step.** The user asked to "add a solo box" after a look at how Clone Hero handles its own, and to "change 5 ticks to 10 ms". Clone Hero 1.1.0.6142's solo box was read from the game's code that session: during a solo it shows the percent of the solo's notes hit, `trunc(float(h) / float(N) * 100.0f)`; after the last note it shows a message for 1.5 s, then the points for 1.5 s, then hides; at 100% the message is "Perfect Solo!" and the bonus is 100 per note. The user's calls in chat:
1. **Content during a solo.** "Game's look + count": the game's percent ("85%"), plus a Hydra line "102 / 120 notes".
2. **After the last note.** "Like the game": "Perfect Solo!" for 1.5 s, then the bonus ("+12,000") for 1.5 s, then the box hides, timed off the last chord so scrubbing shows the same thing. The 1.5 s is the game's (`kSoloResultMs`).
3. **Position.** "Right side, mid-height": right of the highway, a little below the middle, clear of the SP drain and next-activation boxes.
4. **The step.** The step buttons and the comma and period keys move the playhead 10 ms (`kStepMs`). Asked whether it should still pause playback, the user said "Yes, pause".

**D252 (session 77c0ebab), 2026-10-16. The MIDI drum track by either game name, in any case.** Clone Hero 1.1 plays a .mid whose drum track is named "part drums" or "part drum", in any case. Hydra read only the exact name "PART DRUMS", so a chart named "PART DRUM" or "Part Drums" played in the game but failed in Hydra with "No Expert Pro Drums notes in this chart." Hydra now reads both names ignoring case, and the first drum-named track in file order wins, as in the game. The game's code is in `docs/audit/note-shuffle/step0/open1/midi_reader_2155C00.asm` (GameAssembly.dll): it lowercases each track name (`ToLowerInvariant` at 0x2155FDF), compares it exactly with "part drums" (0x2156134) and "part drum" (0x21564A8) with no trim, and its set of instruments already read (`HashSet` Add at 0x21564D7) skips any later drum track. The one test of the name is `is_drums_track_name` in `src/parse/midi.h`; `MidiFile::drums_track` and the track-name pick (D78) ask it. The EVENTS name keeps its exact match. The user's calls in chat:
1. **Saved results.** "No stamp bump." Saved results stay. A chart stuck on Can't analyze is fixed by clicking it, which analyzes it again, or when its file changes.
2. **Track 0.** "Leave it, note it." The game never looks at the first track (index 0) for an instrument (its loop starts at index 1, 0x2155F8D), but Hydra does. That difference was a follow-up; D253 settles it.

What it changes, measured on a copy of the installed hydra.db on 2026-10-16: 19,512 charts, with 59 stored failures, all "No Expert Pro Drums notes in this chart." 13 of them are .chart files and 46 are .mid files. None of the 46 has a "PART DRUM" or case-variant drum track name, so no stored failure in that library changes. Charts that read fine today but hold both names were not counted; one whose "PART DRUM" track comes before its "PART DRUMS" track now reads the first one.

**D253 (session cfb159b5), 2026-10-16. MIDI track 0 is never the drum track, and only the first events track counts.** These are the two MIDI track rules D252 left for later. The game's code is in `docs/audit/note-shuffle/step0/open1/midi_reader_2155C00.asm` (GameAssembly.dll). Its instrument loop starts at track 1 (`mov r15d, 1` at 0x2155F8D), so the first track (index 0) is never read as an instrument, and a single-track (format 0) file has no drums in the game. Its events search starts at track 0 (`xor ebx, ebx` at 0x2155EE4), compares each name with "events" ignoring case (`String.Equals` with comparison type 5, OrdinalIgnoreCase, at 0x2155F30 and 0x2155F36), reads the first match and leaves the loop (0x2155F3D). So only one events track counts, named in any case. Hydra searched every track for drums, compared "EVENTS" exactly, and read practice sections from every events track. Now `MidiFile::drums_track` starts at `MidiFile::kFirstInstrumentTrack`, and the new `MidiFile::events_track` is the one owner of the events track; the song parser and the lean reader both ask it. `is_events_track_name` ignores case, so the track-name pick (D78) lets "Events" win too. A chart whose only drum track is track 0 now reads Can't analyze. The 71 `.mid` charts in `testdata/input` have no drums on track 0, no format 0 file, no other spelling of the events name and no second events track, so no corpus result moves; the crafted format 0 edge file `testdata/parse_edge/m06_format0.mid` now reads no notes. The user's calls in chat:
1. **Track 0.** "Match the game (Recommended)". The drum search starts at track 1.
2. **EVENTS.** "Match the game (Recommended)". The first track named "events" in any case, and only that one.
3. **Saved results.** "No bump (Recommended)". `kResultsStamp` in `src/store/stored_versions.h` stays. An affected chart keeps its old saved result until a click analyzes it again, as in D252.
4. **Library count.** "No, skip the count (Recommended)". How many library charts change was not counted.

**D254 (session 65b16299), 2026-10-17. With Note Shuffle on, a drum lane lights every pad its notes land on.** A drum lane is the coloured strip the Preview draws over a roll or trill. The user's chart "Glide" (Between the Buried and Me) has one single lane over a red snare roll. Note Shuffle turns that roll into yellow cymbals and blue toms in turn, and the game's rule (D112) lights only the pad of the first note, so the lane showed only blue. The user reported that Clone Hero also draws only blue there, and ruled: "clone hero only draws the blue. this will be an intentional deviation from the game. it looks better with both." The user's calls in chat:
1. **Which pads.** "Every pad its notes use." A lane lights every column that a note inside it lands on. Kicks never light a column. A yellow tom and a yellow cymbal share the yellow column.
2. **When.** "Only with Note Shuffle on." With Note Shuffle off, lanes still follow D112, Clone Hero's rule, exactly as before.

`light_drum_lanes` in `src/parse/song.cpp` stays the one owner. The loader tail passes it the Note Shuffle switch, and both modes read the same notes (`lane_span`). This changes only what the Preview draws; nothing in the search or the stored record reads a lane.

**D255 (session c433b17f), 2026-10-18. A song's length reads the chart's end event, as Clone Hero does.** This amends D75 items 1 and 2. The question was where a song ends when the chart's metadata gets the length wrong. The user opened Iron Maiden - Rime Of The Ancient Mariner (Rh) in the Preview. Its song.ini says `song_length = 377034` (6:17), but the audio runs 13:41.5, so the scrub bar ended at 6:17 and the activations after it piled up at the right edge. Commit 0fd29fa had already dropped a stated length that ends before the last Expert drum note, which gave 13:23.8. The chart itself says where it ends, though: an `[end]` text event, which Hydra never read. The handoff is `docs/handoffs/2026-10-18-song-length-end-event-handoff.md` and the plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-18-song-length-end-event.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-18-song-length-end-event.md`).

The measurements, each with its source. For Rime, a throwaway MIDI reader in the handoff's session placed each point through the file's own tempo map: the last PART DRUMS note at tick 774,360 (803,783 ms, 13:23.8), `[music_end]` at tick 776,040 (806,603 ms), `[end]` at tick 781,320 (815,463 ms, 13:35.5), and the audio's end at 13:41.464 (the Preview's time box). Rime is in the user's library, so no test depends on it; its last note over all instruments was not measured. In the checked-in corpus (`testdata/input`), a parse in the planning session found an `[end]` on the EVENTS track in 50 of 66 `notes.mid` files and an `E "end"` in 17 of 31 `notes.chart` files. No file spells it any other way, and no file has two. No corpus end event sits before its last note or within one beat of it.

Clone Hero's rule comes from Clone Hero v1.1's `GameAssembly.dll`, decompiled by two read-only scouts with Il2CppDumper and headless Ghidra. Addresses are offsets from the image base 0x180000000. The song ends when the song clock reaches `GameManager.songLength` (`GameManager.Update`, 0xC05A0). The load coroutine (0xD8D80) builds that length as `Min(end event, Max(audio length, last note end)) + 1 s`, and the 1 s is the double 1.0 at 0x3063DC0. The audio length is the longest loaded audio stem (0x36B700). The last note end is the largest start plus sustain over every instrument and difficulty (0x212E130), turned into time through the tempo map with no offset (0x212B9C0). An end event counts only when its tick is at least the last note end plus one beat (0x212C7E0). One beat is the chart's `Resolution` times the song speed over 100, cut to whole ticks (0x212C0B0, 0x210C090), and a tick exactly on that margin counts. In a .mid the EVENTS handler (0x21544E0) takes `[end]` with its brackets. In a .chart the [Events] reader (0x2163170) strips one pair of brackets, so `end` and `[end]` both count; the flag that skips this (`settings+0x2C`) is 0 in ordinary play, because the load coroutine calls `FUN_18035c110(song, 0, 0, 1, 0)`. The first end event in the file wins (setter 0x212E310). `[music_end]` is not among the game's strings, and song.ini `song_length` is never read on this path; the game writes the audio's length into it when it is missing (0x358850), which is why it usually equals the audio length. The main session checked the shape of the length lines and the `FUN_18035c110` call in the decompiled coroutine; the rest is the scouts' reading. A .sng or .srb holds a whole `notes.mid` or `notes.chart`, and Hydra hands it to the same two parsers, so the end event reaches all four formats.

Hydra never opens audio for a length (D75 item 5), so something had to stand in for the audio length. The user's calls, picked from the plan's options:
1. **What stands in for the audio length.** "Option C (Recommended)". With a stated length, the length is the earlier of the end event and the stated length. With no stated length, or one dropped because it ends before the last note, it is the end event if there is one, else the last note. The stated length, moved into chart time by the delay or Offset as D75 item 3 says, sits in the audio's seat. It matches the game whenever the stated length is the game's own record of the audio.
2. **The one-second pad.** "Copy the pad (Recommended)". The length is the call 1 answer plus 1,000 ms, as in the game, so the song ends in Hydra where it ends in Clone Hero.
3. **The last note.** "All instruments". The last note is the latest note end (start plus sustain) in any instrument section at any difficulty, as in Clone Hero. This is the wider parse, not the plan's default, so Hydra keeps no departure here. The check that drops a stated length compares against this note, and the end event's one-beat margin counts from this note's tick. The cost is a wider read on each click that works out a length: the .mid reader keeps note messages on every instrument track, and the .chart reader every section's notes.
4. **The one-beat margin.** "Copy the margin (Recommended)". An end event counts when its tick is at least the last note end's tick plus `Resolution` ticks. Equal counts; one tick less does not. Hydra works at 100% speed, so the beat is the chart's `Resolution` with no new number.
5. **One end everywhere.** Asked later the same day, the user wrote: "the problem is that we have two different rules. there should one rule that every function uses. we already know how clone hero decides the end of the song, we don't need different places deriving different rules." Asked what the Preview does with the audio length it measures, the user chose "Ignore it (Recommended)". So `app::song_length_ms` is the one end of a song. The Preview's playback stops there, and the time box's total, the scrubber's edge and the gold marks end there too (`PreviewTransport::length_ms`). The measured audio end is never an input. With no length at all (no stated length, no end event, no notes), nothing stops the Preview's clock. This replaces D48's playback range (Q25): playback no longer runs to the later of the last note and the audio's end, and audio past the song's end no longer plays.

Worked results under these calls, from the plan's table: Allister - Overrated (end event 150,098 ms before its stated 151,394 ms) becomes 151,098 ms; Thrice - Deadbolt (end event 186,359 ms after its stated 185,087 ms) becomes 186,087 ms; Karnivool - Themata (no end event) becomes 342,971 ms; Rime, whose stated length is dropped, is expected at 816,463 ms (13:36.5), the game's own number, if its end event clears the margin over all instruments. Chaotrope - Senescence (shortened), in `testdata/input/common/Summer Blast _25 Setlist/Tier 5`, has another instrument section running to 314,996 ms, past its stated 277,000 ms and past its last drum note at 270,896 ms, and no end event. So its stated length is dropped and its length becomes 315,996 ms.

This replaces D75 item 1 (the length is the metadata's stated length) and item 2 (the backup is the start of the last Expert drum note) with the rule above. D75 items 3, 4 and 5 stand: the stated length is moved into chart time, one length drives the Paths timeline, the scrub bar and the SP meter, and no audio is opened. Call 5 makes the same length the Preview's playback end. Nothing stored changes, because no length is stored (`store::SongLength`), so no stamp moves. One known difference stays: where a charter's stated length differs from the real audio, Hydra still uses the stated length in the audio's seat.

**D256 (session e5fc6cff), 2026-10-21. A transfer scale less than a thousandth from 1 counts as no scale.** This replaces D14's 1e-9 tolerance. The transfer scale is the ratio of the bar lengths at the SP end and at the activation (`transfer_scale_between` in `src/core/squeeze_rating.cpp`). D14 counted a scale as no scale only within 1e-9 of 1, on the premise that no real multiplier is that close to 1. MIDI files break that premise. They store each tempo in whole microseconds per quarter note, so two bars meant to be the same length can differ by a few parts per million. Tapestry of the Starless Abstract, a chart in the user's library and not in `testdata/input`, shows it. The main session read its tempo map in this session: it switches from 140 BPM in 4/4 (428,571 µs per quarter) to 105 BPM in 6/8 (571,429 µs per quarter) at tick 433,440. A 4/4 bar there lasts 4 x 428,571 = 1,714,284 µs and a 6/8 bar 3 x 571,429 = 1,714,287 µs, so the late scale is 1,714,287 / 1,714,284 = 1.00000175. The Paths tab showed an orange "Frontend timing scales x1.000002 (late) at the SP end." and "(eff. 71.4 ms)" tags that changed nothing a player could see. The user's call in chat: "Count a scale as “no scale” when the difference from 1 is less than .001".

So `is_scaled` in `src/core/squeeze_rating.h` is true exactly when the scale is 0.001 or more away from 1, and `kScaleIdentityTolerance` is 1e-3. A difference of exactly 0.001 counts as scaled; the user confirmed in chat: "1.001 is not a difference of less than .001". The stored double of 1.001 sits a hair under that distance, so `is_scaled` allows for one machine epsilon of storage error (`kScaleStorageAllowance`). The scale line's digit cap, `kScaleIdentityDigits`, follows from the tolerance and is now 3, so a scaled multiplier still never prints as x1.000. `is_scaled` is display-only: it decides a row's eff. figure (`NoteRating::effective_ms`) and the orange flag (`scale_governs`). The search and the stored records never read it, and stored scales keep full precision, so no stamp moves.

**D257 (session ea3cb421), 2026-10-22. A collapsed activation row shows a badge for every timing it has.** This extends D80. The user reported that on "The Age of Betrayal" (Halcyon Way), optimal path `E3+ E2+ 2 2 3`, rows 1 and 2 showed only `early fill 0 ms`, although their notation has a `+` (a squeeze-in). The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-squeeze-badge-beside-early-fill.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-squeeze-badge-beside-early-fill.md`). That `+` is a free squeeze: a squeeze-in at or before the SP end, figure 0 or below. D80 lets a free squeeze onto the row only "when nothing on the activation needs timing", and the zero-slack early fill counts as a timing, so D80 hid the squeeze. The squeeze showed only in the open row. Nobody had ruled "one badge per row"; the one-badge shape came from the 2.0 design.

The user's calls, in chat on 2026-10-22:
1. **A free squeeze beside an early fill.** "Yes, show both". A free squeeze keeps its badge even when the row also has an early fill badge, so the user's rows read `early fill 0 ms` and then `squeeze in -X ms`, both grey. X is the same whole number Copy path prints for that activation.
2. **How many badges.** "One per timing". A row gets one badge per timing it has, in the notation's order: the early fill first, then each squeeze in list order. That is the order Copy path and the Preview already print the figures in. A row holds at most three badges. Each badge is orange by the rule its own line in the open row already uses, so a free squeeze is never orange, and the timeline mark is orange when any of its row's badges is.
3. **Narrow panels.** "Drop to a second line". The badges stay flush right on the row's line when they fit beside the bars. When they would cross the bars, every badge moves below the row's line, inside the same row. No width or wording changes. Call 4 says where they go there.
4. **Where dropped badges go.** "Under the measure". The user saw the first build of call 3 in real Hydra at a narrow panel and said it "looks ugly as hell": the dropped pills touched the first line's text and stayed flush right, lining up with nothing. Now dropped badges start at the measure's column and run left to right in badge order, one pill gap apart, as on the row's line. The gap above them, between their lines and below them is the row's own top padding, the space between the row's top and its first line's text. A badge that would end further right than a flush-right badge starts the next line under the measure, so three of the longest badge wrap at the narrowest panel. Rows whose badges fit beside the bars stay as they were: flush right, one line tall. The rule is `activation_row_layout` in `src/ui/activation_row_layout.h`.
5. **One font on the row.** "All mono". The bars used to be drawn in the main font and the measure in the mono font, from the same top, so the bars sat a few pixels lower than the measure on every row. Now every piece of a row's text is in the mono font: the number in its circle, the notation, the measure, the bars and every badge. The layout measures the bars and the badges in that font too. Mono badges are wider, so at the narrowest panel one longest badge beside an 11-character measure and `12 bars` now goes under the measure too.
6. **Pill width and one owner.** Each pill used to be as wide as its own text, so `early fill 0 ms` was 153 px and `squeeze in -224 ms` 181 px on a real capture, and stacked pills did not line up on the right. The user ruled "One width per row": every pill in a row is as wide as that row's widest badge text plus the padding, so side-by-side pills match and stacked pills share both edges. Shorter text sits at the left of its pill, at the usual inset. Different rows may have different widths. The user also said "Yes, one helper": `src/ui/badge_pill.h` owns the pill's fill, border, corner radius, padding and size, and every pill on the row is drawn through it. The squeeze box in the open row reads its fill and border colours from the same place and keeps its own box shape. The layout picks the row's one pill width and places the pills with it. A single-owner rule (`tests/single_owner/rules/ui/badge-pill.json`) refuses the two colours typed anywhere else. The numbers did not change, so a row with one badge looks exactly as before.

What stays. D80's example still holds: an activation whose only timing is a free squeeze-in 316 ms inside Star Power reads exactly `squeeze in -316 ms`. D51 call 4 and D60 item 2 stay: an early fill with time to spare has nothing to time and gets no badge. Everything else D80 kept on `hardest()` stays there, with its tie rule: the path button, the Path limit, the library filter, the stored hardest ms, the report and the all-0 list still treat a free squeeze as nothing to time. Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves.

**D258 (session db4f286d), 2026-10-22. Every font pick goes through one owner, and the kind of text picks the font.** Hydra drew text in two fonts, the default font and the mono font (Courier Prime), and each draw call chose one for itself. Nothing ruled which text gets which font; the mix came from the September mockup. In two table headers the width was measured in one font and the text drawn in the other. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-font-role-single-owner.md`. A draw now names its role, the kind of text it shows (a path, a figure, a label, a sentence, a title, a control, an overlay), and `font_for` in `src/ui/text_role.h` is the one place that turns a role into a font and size. A single-owner rule (`tests/single_owner/rules/ui/text_role.json`) refuses a raw font pick anywhere else under `src`.

The user's answer, in chat on 2026-10-22: "Approved. Yes to all five recommendations (Q1-Q5)."
1. **Number columns.** Number columns in tables draw in mono: the path report's Score, Acts, Max skip and Hardest ms, the DM report's counts, and the Dynamics and Stars tables' figures, so the window matches its own saved HTML page.
2. **Library search box.** The search box draws in the default font, so a Japanese title typed into it shows as text instead of boxes.
3. **Multiplier squeeze sentence.** The Multiplier squeeze fold's "how to" sentence and its "None." line draw in the default font, like every other squeeze sentence on the Paths tab.
4. **Table headers.** Every table header row draws in the default font and is measured in it, which fixes the two headers measured in mono and drawn in default.
5. **Timeline numbers.** The small activation numbers above the Paths tab timeline draw in mono, matching the numbers in the rows under them.

The row numbers in a report window's "#" column are numbers in a column, so they follow the Figure rule of answer 1: mono cells, a default-font header. Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves.

**D259 (session a8ea987c), 2026-10-22. Hydra checks for its own updates and installs one only when you click Update.** The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-auto-updater.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-auto-updater.md`) (task UPD-P1); its version rule is in `docs/development.md`, "The version rule". Once a day, an installed Hydra asks GitHub for the latest release. If it is newer, a line under the toolbar offers it. Clicking `Update` downloads the installer, checks it against a signature made with the project's key, and starts it.

The user approved the plan in chat on 2026-10-22: "Approve the plan with every recommended decision. Start the build." So every call below is the plan's recommendation. Two choices outside the numbered list came with it. Hydra builds its own updater on its WinHTTP code and the GitHub release page, not WinSparkle. The signature is ECDSA on the P-256 curve with SHA-256, checked through Windows' bcrypt, because Windows' crypto API has no Ed25519.

1. **Where the notice lives.** A one-line strip under the toolbar, where the rules-file warning sits, in the normal text colour. No modal box.
2. **The notice's wording.** `Hydra Deluxe 2.11.0 is available.` (the release tag without its `v`), with the buttons on the same line. Hovering the version shows the release's notes as plain text.
3. **The buttons.** `Update`, `Skip this version`, `Later`. Skip writes the version to the INI and hides it only; a newer release shows again. Later hides the strip until the next check.
4. **The progress display.** The strip reads `Downloading Hydra Deluxe 2.11.0...  12.3 of 33.2 MB` with a bar and a `Cancel` button, then `Checking the download...`, then `Starting the installer...`. Megabytes are bytes divided by 1,000,000, to one decimal. Cancel deletes the part-file and returns to call 2's state.
5. **The setting.** A checkbox labelled `Check for updates`, default on, with the help text "Once a day, when Hydra starts, look for a newer release on GitHub. Nothing is downloaded or installed until you click Update." It sits in a new fourth group of the Analysis settings panel named `App`, after `Paths kept`, with a `Check now` button. The batch lock never greys it out and `Reset to defaults` never touches it, because it is not part of a result's profile.
6. **How often.** At most once per 24 hours, counted from the last finished check and stored in the INI. `Check now` ignores the 24 hours.
7. **Timeouts and the size cap.** The download reuses the four WinHTTP timeouts the leaderboard client already sets. The size cap is the release asset's own `size` field. No new number.
8. **Silent failures.** The automatic daily check says nothing on any failure and tries again the next day. A failure after `Check now` or `Update` shows in the strip through `plain_error` and `plain_error_detail`.
9. **The refusal sentence.** Every refusal (a missing or doubled asset, no signature, no digest, a body past the stated size, a wrong digest, a signature that fails) reads "Hydra couldn't verify the update it downloaded, so it was not installed. Try again later, or download the release from GitHub yourself." One error kind, `UpdateUnverified`; the details line names the check that failed. Hydra never installs anyway.
10. **A declined permission prompt.** Hydra keeps running and the strip reads `The installer didn't start, so Hydra wasn't updated.` with call 3's buttons back. The download is kept for the retry and deleted at the next start.
11. **The "updated" message.** The first start after any version change reads `Updated to Hydra Deluxe 2.11.0.` with an `OK` button, and the INI's `last_run_version` is set. It fires after a manual install too, so no installer flag is needed.
12. **Where the private key lives.** `%USERPROFILE%\.hydra-release\update-signing.xml`, protected to the user's Windows account, outside the repo and never in CI. The user backs it up in their password store.
13. **The public wording.** The README, after the SmartScreen sentence: "Hydra Deluxe checks for a newer release once a day when it starts and tells you under the toolbar. Click `Update` to download and install it; nothing is installed until you do. To stop the check, untick `Check for updates` in `Analysis settings`." The User Guide says the same in its Toolbar section, adds the `Check for updates` row to the settings table and one sentence to "The first start after updating". The release note says it in one paragraph.
14. **Which builds check.** Only a Hydra.exe in the folder the installer registered. The one rule is `app::installed_by_setup()`: the exe's folder equals `InstallLocation` under the Inno uninstall key for Hydra's frozen AppId, compared case-insensitively with trailing separators removed. Dev, worktree and copied builds never check.

**The ruling on how Hydra starts the installer.** An executor found, in Inno Setup's source, that the installer's relaunch of Hydra runs as the original user only when Setup raises its own UAC prompt. If Hydra started Setup already elevated (the `runas` verb), the relaunch would run Hydra as administrator. The user chose, in chat on 2026-10-22: "Use the recommended fix: start the installer normally, Inno asks for admin, Hydra stays open until the installer closes it, and a declined prompt keeps Hydra running with decision 10's message." So Hydra starts the installer with the plain `open` verb and Inno's silent switches, and keeps its process handle. If the user accepts the prompt, the installer closes Hydra through Windows Restart Manager and opens it again when it finishes. If the installer's process ends while Hydra still runs, the update did not happen, and the strip shows call 10's text. The installer's launch line in `installer/hydra.iss` drops `skipifsilent`, so it runs on the silent install too; the file's design notes say why the verb must stay `open`.

**Six rulings during the build.** The build raised these questions, and each time the user picked the recommended option in chat on 2026-10-22. (1) Update-check network failures name GitHub, not dmleaderboards: `Hydra couldn't reach GitHub. Check your internet connection and try again.`, `GitHub didn't answer in time. Try again later.`, `GitHub sent a reply Hydra couldn't read. Try again later.` and `GitHub returned an error (HTTP <code>). Try again later.` (2) A `Check now` that finds nothing newer shows `Hydra Deluxe <version> is the latest version.` in the strip with an `OK` button; the daily check stays silent. (3) A `last_update_check` time that is unreadable or in the future counts as due. (4) An installer that fails to start shows call 10's state, `The installer didn't start, so Hydra wasn't updated.` with the three buttons back, and the Windows error as the detail line. (5) `Check now` ignores the skipped version and offers that release again, because a click means you want to know; the daily check keeps honoring the skip. (6) The failed strip gets a `Later` button, plus `Update` when a release was found, and `Later` hides it until the next check. The User Guide's "When a new version is out" says all six.

**The ruling on Check now outside the installed copy.** As first built, `Check now` skipped call 14: in a dev or copied build it checked, and `Update` could then run the real installer over the user's install. The user ruled in chat on 2026-10-22: "Yes: in a build the installer didn't put in place, grey out Check now with the hover text "Only the installed copy of Hydra Deluxe checks for updates." and make check_updates_now refuse too." So `AppState::check_updates_now` asks `app::installed_by_setup()` and does nothing when it says no, and the App group's `Check now` button is greyed out with that hover text (task UPD-T5).

The owners of the four questions the updater asks are listed under "Update check" in CONTEXT.md. Nothing stored in `hydra.db` changes, so no stamp moves.

**D260 (session b4311ae5), 2026-10-23. Every start checks for updates, and a version bar tops the window.** This supersedes parts of D259. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-update-check-on-launch.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-update-check-on-launch.md`) (tasks UPD2-P1 and UPD2-P2). The installed Hydra now asks GitHub for the latest release each time it starts, with no switch and no wait between checks. A new bar along the top of the window, above the toolbar, always names the running version and says whether a newer one is out, and everything about updates shows there.

The user's rulings, in chat on 2026-10-23, in their words: "the app should automatically check for updates when it launches. it shouldnt need tyhe user to manually check. a version label is good too." On an off switch: "No off switch". On the first draft, which put a dimmed version label in the toolbar: "why are you still trying ot make this part of analysis settings? the whole point is for version to be its own prominent thing". From three mocks: "Go with A, the version bar along the top of the window."

The answers to the plan's four questions, in the user's words:

1. **What `Later` leaves behind: "Keep an Update button".** After `Later` the bar keeps the amber dot, `Hydra Deluxe <new> is available.` and its `Update` button. Only `Skip this version` and `Later` go, until the next start. `Later` still writes nothing to the INI.
2. **What a skip shows: "Available, marked skipped".** While the skipped release is the latest, every start shows the amber dot and `Hydra Deluxe <new> is available.` with `Skipped.` dimmed after it, and no buttons. A newer release is offered as before.
3. **Where the "updated" note goes: "In the top bar".** `Updated to Hydra Deluxe <version>.` with its `OK` takes the bar's sentence slot. The start-up check still runs behind it, and `OK` gives the slot to what the check found: the offer for a newer release that is not skipped, or the resting sentence.
4. **Which states get a dot: "Grey dot for the rest".** Green means up to date and amber means a newer release is known. Every other state shows a grey dot, so the bar always has one.

What was removed: the `App` group of the `Analysis settings` panel, its `Check for updates` checkbox and its `Check now` button; the 24-hour wait between checks (`update_check_due` and `kUpdateCheckInterval`); the up-to-date strip with its `OK`, which only `Check now` ever showed; the update strip under the toolbar, whose drawing moved into the bar; and the INI keys `check_updates` and `last_update_check`, which join `kRetiredSettingsKeys` so an old file loads and loses them at its next save. A player who unticked `Check for updates` in 2.11.x gets the check again, as "No off switch" rules. The release note says so.

The bar's words come from `AppState::version_bar_status`, and the bar only draws them. With no update step in progress it reads, in order: `Only the installed copy of Hydra Deluxe checks for updates.` dimmed, in a copy the installer didn't put in place; `Checking for updates...` while the check runs; `Hydra couldn't check for updates when it started.` with the check's `plain_error` sentence dimmed after it when it failed; `Hydra Deluxe <new> is available.` with an amber dot after `Later` or a skip; otherwise `Up to date` with a green dot. During an update step the bar shows that step's sentence and buttons, unchanged from D259; the offer carries the amber dot. The name on the left is `Hydra Deluxe <version>` from `core::named_version(core::running_version())`, the one text of a version, in a new text role, `TextRole::AppVersion` (the default font at 20 px). The name has no click. Three new theme colours each have one owner in `src/ui/theme.h`: `kVersionBarBg` for the bar and `kUpToDateDotColor` and `kUpdateAvailableDotColor` for the two dots, and `kNeutralDotColor` for the grey dot of answer 4.

Superseded in D259: call 5 (the setting and the `App` group), call 6 (once per 24 hours), call 8 (the silent daily check; a failed start-up check now shows in the bar) and the build rulings (2) (the `Check now` up-to-date line) and (5) (`Check now` ignoring the skip). The ruling on `Check now` outside the installed copy has nothing left to govern; its sentence now shows in the bar instead. The owners of the three questions the updater asks are listed under "Update check" in CONTEXT.md. Nothing stored in `hydra.db` changes, so no stamp moves.

The public docs were checked with this grep of `README.md`, `docs/UserGuide.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, the new paragraph of `docs/releases/release-next-notes.md` and `docs/agents/ui-testing.md` for `Check now`, `Check for updates`, `once a day`, `24 hours`, `check_updates` and `under the toolbar`. The hits left are history or not about updates. The new release-notes paragraph names the removed `Check for updates` box and `Check now` button on purpose, to say they are gone, and its "no longer sits under the toolbar" says where the line used to be. The guide's three "under the toolbar" lines are about the status message and the batch strip, which stay there. The shipped release-notes paragraph above the new one is history and keeps its words.

**D261 (session 6fc3d247), 2026-10-24. An early fill with time to spare gets its badge too.** The user reported that on Weltseele, activation 13 (`E2+`), the open row says "Early fill: -56.2 ms (optional)" but the collapsed row shows only `squeeze in -56 ms`. The fill's badge was missing because a fill with time to spare got none. The user ruled, in chat on 2026-10-24: "the fix is to remove the exception and make the badge always show."

So every E-critical activation (`Activation::is_e_critical`, the same test that puts the `E` in the notation and the "Early fill:" line in the open row) lists its early fill in `Activation::badge_timings`, whatever the sign of its figure. A fill with time to spare reads like `early fill -56 ms` and is never orange, the same as a free squeeze (D80). The order stays: the fill first, then each squeeze. An activation that is not E-critical still gets no fill badge, because it shows no fill figure anywhere. This replaces the badge half of D60 item 2 and the "fill with time to spare" exclusion D257 kept under "What stays".

What stays. D51 call 4 still holds for everything but the badge: a fill with time to spare is nothing to time. `Activation::hardest` asks `early_fill_needs_timing` before it weighs a fill, so such a fill is never named hardest, and `difficulty()`, `needs_timing()`, `within_ms_limit()`, the path buttons, the Path limit and the search behave as before. Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves.

**D262 (session 77f5bfec), 2026-10-24. Every badge pill is one width, its text is centred, and one helper draws every badge.** This replaces D257 call 6's "One width per row". Under that call each row's pills shared that row's widest badge, so pill edges jumped from row to row and short text sat at the left of its pill. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-24-badge-pill-one-width.md` (never committed; not kept) (task PILL-W1). The user ruled, in chat on 2026-10-24: "go ahead with longest fixed width, center aligned in the badge. there should be one shared helper function that all badges draw from."

The user's calls:
1. **One width.** "longest fixed width". Every timing badge's pill, on every row of every chart, is as wide as the longest badge Hydra can show plus the usual padding each side. That badge is `longest_activation_badge()` in `src/app/path_view.cpp`. The Paths tab measures it once in the Figure role and passes it to `activation_row_layout` (`src/ui/activation_row_layout.h`), which sizes every pill with it. A badge wider than that would grow its pill, so a pill is never narrower than its text. No new number: the padding is the one D257 call 6 already used. On the GUI test harness (`hydra_uitest --test paths-row-layout`) the longest badge's text measures 183 px, so every pill is 199 px.
2. **Centred.** "center aligned in the badge". The badge text sits in the middle of its pill. `centred_text_x` in `src/ui/badge_pill.h` places it.
3. **One helper.** "one shared helper function that all badges draw from". `draw_badge` in `src/ui/badge_pill.h` draws a whole badge: the pill, then its text centred, in the Figure role and the colour the caller gives (orange or grey, by the rule D257 call 2 kept). `render_activation_row` in `src/ui/paths_tab.cpp` draws badges only through it. A second row in `tests/single_owner/rules/ui/badge-pill.json` refuses a badge pill drawn or its text placed anywhere else under `src`.

What stays. D257 calls 1 to 5 stand, and so does the colour half of call 6: `badge_pill.h` owns the pill's fill and border, and the squeeze box in the open row keeps reading them and keeps its own shape. The drop rule (calls 3 and 4) does not change, but a wider pill reaches the bars sooner, so on a narrow panel a row whose short badge used to fit beside the bars can now drop under the measure. The library's status chips and the report windows' chips are other widgets and do not change. Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves.

**D263 (session dcae32ba), 2026-10-24. The library scans, stores, shows and searches each chart's album.** Before this, Hydra read a chart's title, artist and charter but not its album, so a player could not find a song by its album. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-24-album-search.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-24-album-search.md`) (tasks ALB-1 to ALB-5). The user made ten calls in chat on 2026-10-24:

1. **A column.** The full-size library gets an `Album` column between `Artist` and `Charter`.
2. **Its width.** Album's share comes out of Folder only: Title 17, Artist 10, Album 14, Charter 9, Folder 25, Best path 25 (`library_column_specs` in `src/ui/library_table.cpp`). A real-app check on the user's library settles the final split before the table merges.
3. **The second line.** Whenever the Album column is hidden and Artist is shown, every row is two lines tall and shows its album under the Artist column, search or not. A blank album leaves that line empty. During a word search the album's match is in gold and the footer counts it (`Matched on album.`); with no search there is no highlight and no footer. The condition is Album hidden, not the panel open, so an album turned back on from the header menu sits in its column and is not repeated. The line under the title (folder, or `charted by …`) still shows only during a word search. `render_table` in `src/ui/library_table.cpp` holds the rule. The user ruled on 2026-10-25 that the album line shows on every row whenever Album is hidden: "with a song open, every row shows its album under the artist, search or not". The first version showed it only during a search, which narrowed their original ask, "make a new line under the artist column when the path is expanded? like Artist X / Album Y".
4. **A key.** A new `album:` key beside `artist:`, `charter:` and `folder:`, and plain words match the album too. The keys are listed in the Search tips popup (call 10), not in a hint line.
5. **A blank album.** A chart with no album shows an empty cell and never matches; it gets no placeholder like the artist's `(unknown)`. The album is cleaned the way an artist is, by `display_album` in `src/parse/song.h`.
6. **Where it comes from.** The scan reads `album` from song.ini, from a `.sng`'s metadata and from a `.srb`'s header (`SrbMetadata::album`). A `.chart` file's own `[Song] Album` is not read, because the scan reads no `.chart` names at all.
7. **Stored.** The `charts` table gains an `album` column, and an older library file gains it in place on open. `kChartMetaStamp` goes from 5 to 6, so the next scan reads every chart's names again once. No result is analyzed again.
8. **Out of scope.** The report windows, `hydra_batch`, the song panel, and grouping by album.
9. **The guide.** The User Guide's search section names the `album:` key and the `Album` column, and the docs-vs-code test keeps passing.
10. **Search tips.** The long hint line under the search box goes away (option B from the mock). In its place is one short line, `Type any words, or narrow it down.`, ending in a `Search tips` link. The link opens a small popup with one key per line: an example, then what it does. The box's placeholder becomes `Search songs`. New keys go into the popup, so the line under the box never grows again. The list lives once, in `search_tips()` in `src/app/library_query.h`.

The plan asked three questions, and the user answered all three in chat on 2026-10-24: "approve with recommended."

1. **Saved column state.** Every existing `LibrarySort` value keeps its number and Album takes the next free one, `LibrarySort::Album` = 5, at position 2. The plan's reason was wrong: a saved `hydra_ui.ini` matches each column by a hash of its header label (`TableSetupColumn`, `third_party/imgui/imgui_tables.cpp` line 1752), not by the user ID, so inserting Album could never move a player's hidden columns or sort. The IDs were kept anyway because they keep the sort round trip clean. The real-app check on the user's library copy (2026-10-24) confirmed the saved Title sort came through and Album started visible.
2. **The song panel.** Album hides and comes back with the panel, beside Charter and Folder. The narrow view keeps Title, Artist and Best path, and the album moves to the second line.
3. **Sorting.** The Album header sorts like every other name column. This replaces the "sorting by album" half of call 8.

The user also checked what `stars:N` means. The code keeps a row only when its best path earns exactly N stars (`query_matches`). In the app, the user's own library showed about 12.5k charts at `stars:7` and about 7k at `stars:6`. The user judged that right, since a chart with no Star Power can't reach 7 stars. So the guide and the Search tips say "best path", which is what the filter reads, where the guide used to say "optimal path".

**D264 (session 9e7440c1), 2026-10-25. A cancelled network call must return within 500 ms.** The cancel tests in `tests/test_dm_report.cpp` hold a request on a loopback server that never answers, set the cancel flag, and time how long the call runs on. `check_cancelled_promptly` fails the test at 500 ms or more. The transport itself checks the flag every 50 ms (the wait loop in `src/net/http.cpp`), so the limit leaves room for a slow machine. The test-suite audit asked whether to tighten it, because a cancel check slowed from 50 ms to 450 ms would still pass; a deliberately slowed build measured 438 ms and passed. The user chose in chat on 2026-10-25, picking "Keep 500 ms (Recommended)" over "200 ms over 5 cancels", knowing that. The audit's report is `docs/handoffs/2026-10-25-test-audit/report.md` (never committed; it stays untracked in the main checkout while its follow-ups are open).

**D266 (session caca5d75), 2026-10-29. Four mismatches settled: a strict `squeeze<`, a missing charter, the Preview's beat lines and a manual scan over a missing folder.** Each was a place where Hydra did two alike things two different ways. The user made four calls in chat on 2026-10-29, each through a question whose options showed what the code really did. Other agents build calls 2 to 4; this entry is their record.

1. **`squeeze<` is strict, like `>`.** The user chose "Strict, like >". Before this, `parse_squeeze` in `src/app/library_query.cpp` read `squeeze<N` as `squeeze<=N`, so `squeeze<20` kept a chart whose hardest squeeze or required early fill was exactly 20 ms while `squeeze>20` dropped it. Now `squeeze<20` drops a chart at exactly 20 ms and keeps one at 19.9 ms. `squeeze<=20` still keeps 20 ms, and `>` and `>=` do not change. The parser sets `LibraryQuery::squeeze_max_strict` for `<` the way it sets `squeeze_min_strict` for `>`, and `query_matches` asks the negation of `timing_reaches_floor` for it. A path that needs no timing still passes any limit, strict or not. The Search tips, the User Guide and `CONTEXT.md` say so. This replaces the "`squeeze<N` means at most N" item of D51 call 13 (finding 323), which kept the old behaviour as "today's behaviour with no decision behind it" (`docs/audit/2026-10-04-phase-7-questions.md`, question 13).
2. **A missing charter reads `(unknown)`, like the artist and the title.** This replaces the charter part of D27.
3. **The Preview's beat lines stop at the song's end.** That end is `song_length_ms`, the one end D255 call 5 made every part of the Preview use. This extends D255 and replaces D48 Q25's "the beat lines run to the end of the audio".
4. **A manual `Scan library` refuses when a chart folder is missing, like the scan on open.** This closes the out-of-scope line of the rescan-on-open plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-25-rescan-on-open.md`, never committed; not kept), which left the manual scan writing an empty library over a missing folder (D87 item 4) for the user to decide.

Nothing stored changes for call 1, so no stamp moves.

**D268 (session caca5d75), 2026-10-29. `hydra_batch` refuses a missing chart folder too, and a manual refusal has its own sentence.** These follow D266 call 4. The user made two more calls in chat on 2026-10-29.

1. **The batch tool refuses, like the app.** The user chose "Refuse, like the app (Rec.)". Before this, a whole-library `hydra_batch` run saved its scan as the library through `save_scan_as_library` even when a chart folder was missing, and printed the folder's error only after the save. Now such a run stops before it opens the store, scans, saves or analyzes anything. It prints the notice naming the folder and exits with code 2. A run over folders passed by hand never saves the library, so it does not change. The one check of which chart folder is missing is `app::first_missing_chart_folder` in `src/app/analysis.cpp`, beside the scan code. The app's `AppState::scan_blocked_by_missing_folder` and the batch tool both call it.
2. **A manual refusal has its own sentence.** The user chose "Its own sentence (Rec.)". A scan the user starts, and the batch tool, say "<folder> cannot be read, so nothing was scanned. Click Scan library when it is back." The scan on open keeps "Your library needs a scan, but <folder> cannot be read. Click Scan library when it is back." One owner, `app::library_scan_blocked_notice` in `src/app/user_messages.cpp`, is told which scan asked (`app::ScanAsker`) and writes both, with the shared second sentence once. Neither call named the scan Hydra starts after the set-aside box closes. The reviewer settled it: Hydra starts that scan on its own and the library really does need it, so it counts as a scan at open. `startup_notice_closed` now asks through `request_rescan_on_open`, the same path as an out-of-date library, which sets `scan_started_by_open`, and `start_scan` gives the open's sentence to the scan the open asked for. One visible side effect: the `Scanning charts` window for that scan now opens with the line "An older version of Hydra scanned this library. Scanning again to read what this version adds.", as it does for an out-of-date library. A unit test pins both the flag and the sentence.
3. **One owner for which folder is missing.** A single-owner row (`rules/app/d268-missing-chart-folder.json`) flags any other `is_directory_utf8` call in `src/` or `tools/`, so a second loop over the chart folders cannot appear unnoticed.

Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves.

**D269 (session 61cee421), 2026-10-30. Every Backend timings row is rated by one rule, on the timing the player needs.** The audit of the past audits found two rating ladders on the Paths tab's Backend timings rows: one for plain rows and one for the squeezed-out row, with different words and edges. The user's calls in chat on 2026-10-30, in their words:
1. **One rule.** Asked whether every row should use one rating rule, with the same labels and edges, squeezed out or not: "yes everything should use one rule."
2. **The timing the player needs, not the raw offset.** Rejecting a first design that rated rows by their raw offset: "what really matters here is the effective timing for the player, not the absolute offset. hydra already shows this with the badges next to each activation. -100 for a backend and a squeeze out are not the same thing"
3. **The report's scale.** On rating the rows on the report's timing tiers: "the report scale sounds good".
4. **One Star Power end.** "there should never be two different answers for where star power ends. ever. the 3ms rule is for whether a backend is included in the optimal score or uncounted. it's an arbitrary number chosen by dragondelgar and i am fine with keeping it so our scores use the same methodology as delgar's hydra". The 3 ms leeway is DragonDelgar's (public Hydra's `hydra/hypath.py`, line 678 at his commit 3b49012c), not Hydra Deluxe's own choice.
5. **Badges and the report stay on raw ms.** "keep the badges and reports as they are. if you made them use the effective figure then you'd need to remove the timing scale text or users would understandably think those multipliers need to be aplied again"

The design is `docs/audit/2026-10-29-audit-of-audits/blocks/AOA-R2-design.md`, read with calls 2 to 5. Its first draft, `AOA-R1-design.md`, treats the leeway as a second Star Power end and is rejected by call 4. Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves.

**D270 (session 9a82ff65), 2026-10-30. Settings and rules files: one range rule, anything bad refuses, and one fixed 85 ms hit window.** Group 1 of the audit-of-audits questions (`docs/audit/2026-10-29-audit-of-audits/questions.md`, Q1 to Q4), plus the follow-ups they raised. Each question showed what the code did through every door, with examples. The user's calls in chat on 2026-10-30:
1. **An SP cap below 1 reads 1 through every door (Q1).** The user chose "Nearest edge (Recommended)". `sp_cap=0` or below in the file reads 1, as the Settings box already does, and `hydra_replay --cap 0` reads 1 instead of refusing. This replaces `Outside::DefaultFromFile`, the D51 addendum's "0, junk and `auto` still read as 4" and its code-only "`--cap 0` still refuses on the command line".
2. **Every out-of-range number lands on its nearest edge (Q2).** The user chose "Nearest edge for all (Recommended)". `depth_mode=7` reads 1. This replaces the D51 addendum's "depth_mode out of range reads as the default".
3. **The five `hydra_rules.ini` floors stay (Q3).** After each key and its floor were explained: "yes those floors are fine". They are `backend_leeway_ms` 0, `max_tied_paths` 1, `fill_cooldown_measures` 1, `fill_max_distance_beats` 0 and `fill_land_slop_beats` 0 (`rules_fields` in `src/core/rules.cpp`).
4. **Anything bad refuses, in both files (Q4).** The user chose "Both refuse (Recommended)", then: "refuse anything bad". A line with no `=`, a number that is not a clean number, an on/off key that is not 0 or 1, and a difficulty word Hydra does not know all stop Hydra with an error that names the file, the key and the value, in `hydra_settings.ini` as in `hydra_rules.ini`. `sp_cap=abc` no longer reads 4, `view_prodrums=yes` no longer keeps the old value and `view_difficulty=Expret` no longer reads Expert. This replaces D67 (junk after a number reads the default) and the settings file's "junk reads as 0".
5. **An unknown key refuses.** The user chose "Refuse it (Recommended)" for a key Hydra does not know, such as a typo.
6. **Old versions get no support.** "we dont need to support old versions". `sp_cap=auto`, which 1.8.4 wrote, is junk under call 4 and refuses.
7. **Keys Hydra used to write are skipped and dropped.** Told that every player's settings file holds `hit_window_ms=85`, a line Hydra wrote itself, the user chose "Skip and drop them". A key on Hydra's own list of keys it used to write (`kRetiredSettingsKeys`, `kRetiredRulesKeys`) is skipped on load and left out the next time Hydra saves. This is the one exception to call 5.
8. **One hit window number everywhere, fixed at 85.** The user asked "why are there two different numbers for hit window? who approved that? did you ever tell me you were using two different numbers?", then "yes it should use one nuimbers eerywhere", and chose "Fixed 85, no setting (Recommended)". Before this, the `hit_window_ms` setting (default 85, `kDefaultHitWindowMs`) fed the squeeze budgets, the Rating words and the report's timing tiers, while the search read its own fixed 85 (`kEarlyFillReachMs`, D250 call 2). The setting had no box and was never put to the user as a setting: it came with the 2026-08-20 "Transfer-Aware Squeezes" build (commit d49c6243), where the user said "the hit window is +/- 85ms, the 70ms is an outdated figure" and chose "Yes, 85 everywhere (Recommended)". Now one constant is the hit window for the badges, ratings, tiers and the search, and `hit_window_ms` joins the retired keys (call 7). This replaces D250 call 2's "not the hit window setting, which stays display-only".

The search's number stays 85, so no path or score changes and no stamp moves. How a refused settings file looks on screen at start is a display change, so its design goes to the user in the build's plan.

**D271 (session 9a82ff65), 2026-10-30. Audit-of-audits Groups 2 to 4: two questions dropped, six settled.** From `docs/audit/2026-10-29-audit-of-audits/questions.md`. Before each question went out, the main session re-read the code it quotes on main that day. The user's calls in chat:
1. **Q5 and Q6 are dropped, because no real chart is behind them.** Q5 asked why a `.srb` reads the bytes of an oddly named notes stream while a `.sng` goes by name only. Q6 asked why loose files count as audio by name while `.srb` streams count by their bytes. The user asked "is there any real chart where this actually matters or are you just inventing situations to waste my time", then "point me to a real chart where this could be an issue, not one you created, and i'll consider it". No real container with an odd notes name, and no `.srb` holding a preview clip, has been found. Both stay as the code has them, and neither is asked again unless a real chart shows one.
2. **Q7 waits on a live Clone Hero check.** Hydra's rule is that a chart with any written fill gets no generated fills (`Song::check_activations`). On the test charts, 66 charts have written fills. None has only one, but some go a long way without one. Offered a check of DOMi & JD Beck's "Zildjian LIVE! Performance 2020", whose last 73 measures have no written fill, the user said "Yes, check it". In that chart only one Star Power phrase ends after its last fill, so Star Power never becomes ready in the stretch and a run there can't show anything. So the main session moved the check to Senri Kawaguchi's "Ladies Talk" (`testdata/input/common/IB24/T6/`). Its written fills skip a drum solo from measure 76 to 108, and two or more phrases end inside the solo, so Star Power becomes ready inside it. The check needs the `ch` server to read Star Power and to start a song for the auto-player, which the plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-ch-mcp-star-power-and-song-start.md` adds. The game's bot never uses Star Power, so a bot run can't settle it.
3. **One "nothing matches" wording (Q8).** The user chose "One wording (Recommended)". The Library ("No charts match your search.", hint "Search songs") and the report windows ("Nothing matches those filters.", hints "Search song, artist, or charter" and "..., or path notation") get one sentence and one hint pattern. The pattern names what the box searches, and adds path notation where a window can search it. The sentence uses neither "search" nor "filters".
4. **Time left comes from the rate over recent work, in both places (Q10).** The user chose "Recent rate, both (Recommended)". Before this, the batch strip divided the time so far by the charts finished (`kEtaMinFinished` 3), while the Preview load and the startup screen used the rate over the last second, shown after 3 s (`kTimeLeftAfterSeconds`). For the batch, the user chose "Measure, then pick" for the window: an agent times a batch on the test charts with each candidate window and shows the user the estimates before one is chosen.
5. **The batch strip's dot gets one space each side (Q11).** The user chose "One space everywhere (Recommended)". The strip's first dot uses `kFactSeparator` like every other line. The Paths tab's leading `· Backends ...` is a list marker, not a separator, and stays.
6. **One "unknown option" line (Q12).** The user chose "One line, one helper (Recommended)". `hydra_batch`, `hydra_fillcompare`, `hydra_replay` and `hydra_precheck` print the same line from one helper. `hydra_replay` still prints its usage after it.
7. **Both report windows keep their rows when closed (Q13).** The user chose "Both keep". Told that this reverses D103 item 28, the user confirmed: "Yes, both keep". D103 item 28 was the user's own choice on 2026-10-08, made after a closed path report was measured holding about 42 MB (68,037 rows) on the user's library. Now closing the path report only hides it, as the comparison window already did, and reopening is instant. Two follow-ons of item 28 go with it: a build still running when the window closes is no longer cancelled, and after a close the batch strip keeps "The path report is ready." and its "Open report" button. The 2026-10-09 follow-up that keeps the window's search, timing dropdown, "Best path only" and sort across a reopen still holds.
8. **The toolbar's Compare button works at any SP cap (Q14).** The user chose "Button always on (Recommended)". Before this, at cap 8 the toolbar refused ("Needs SP cap 4, Clone Hero's rule: ...") while the open window's Refresh, Try again and Compare another built at cap 4 (`profile_at_clone_hero_cap`). Now the button works at any cap, and the page says it compares at cap 4, Clone Hero's rule. Difficulty still refuses as D114 item 6 says. This replaces the auditor's word "deliberate" in D114 Q6 for the cap half.

Nothing stored changes, so no stamp moves. Items 3 to 8 change what the app shows, so each build shows its design to the user first.

**D272 (sessions 9a82ff65 and 4c48c8e5), 2026-10-30. The `ch` server may drive menus, call game methods and write game memory, and a live run needs nobody at the keyboard.** From the plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-ch-mcp-star-power-and-song-start.md`, "Rulings recorded". Two of the rules it replaces, "no tool opens any menu" and "never write game memory, set a field or call a game method", came into the 2026-10-28 server plan inside a batch approval ("Yes, all seven"); the user never picked either one on its own. 3. **The menu numbers.** The user chose "Use them (Recommended)": 10 s per menu step, 0.25 s between keys, at most 10 Down presses in the search, a menu read every 0.5 s, and a run cap of the song length plus 60 s (else 240 s).

The user says in chat when a run may start; the run holds the window focus, and the server's backup and restore put the files back. Later on 2026-10-30, ruling 1 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-ch-mcp-in-game-input.md` overruled the focus part of this item: the user said "yes we need to do autonomous runs. that waws the whole point of this", so a run never focuses or shows the game window and feeds its input from inside the game.

The plan's "Rulings recorded" section, copied word for word on 2026-10-31 so this record does not lean on the plan. Its heading is set in bold so it stays under this record:

**Rulings recorded (2026-10-30)**

The first version of this plan asked four questions, and a second round asked two more. The user answered all of them, and these answers are now settled; nothing below re-asks them.

1. On the rule "no tool opens any menu" (`docs/agents/clone-hero-mcp.md`, "Safety rules"), the user said: "nobody picked that rule. create a way to navigate menus if it doesnt exist." So the key-navigation route is built, and task T5 rewrites that rule as the "Rule rewrites" section says.
2. On the rule "never write game memory, set a field or call a game method", the user said: "again, nobody selected this rule. figure out the correct method." So calling a game method is allowed, and this plan finds the method as far as the dump lets it and designs the live study that settles it. Ruling 5 covers writing memory and setting fields.
3. On the four menu numbers and the run cap, the user chose "Use them (Recommended)": `MENU_STEP_CAP_S = 10`, `MENU_KEY_GAP_S = 0.25`, `SEARCH_DOWN_CAP = 10`, `MENU_POLL_S = 0.5`, and the run cap of song length plus `bot_run.POLL_CAP_EXTRA_S` (60 s) when the folder is known, else 240 s.
4. On the first menu key sequence, the user chose "Data file, fixed live (Recommended)": T6 writes `tools/ch_mcp/menu_steps.json` from the manual, marked unverified, and LS1 corrects it in a one-file data commit.
5. On writing game memory and setting fields (the test `test_no_agent_writes_to_the_game` in `tools/ch_mcp/tests/test_frida_attach.py` and the third paragraph of the agent page's "Safety rules"), the user chose "Allow writes too": agents may set game fields where a feature needs it. If `ch_start_song` needs a field set, it may set it.

There are no open questions in this plan.

The questions, in full. The session's card keeps only the start of each question next to the answer; the rest was copied on 2026-10-31 from the session's condensed transcript (a local working file, not kept in the repo) before the cards moved into it. Times are UTC.
- 23:32 UTC. Q: When a squeezed-out row is 3 ms or more past the SP end, the engine counts it as 0 points. What should the details table say on that row instead of '260 ... (-200)'? Answer: chose "0 + "(uncounted)" (Recommended)"
- 23:32 UTC. Q: Should the engine's own search loop also call the new shared backend-pricing function, or only the replay and the display? Answer: chose "Engine too (Recommended)"
- 23:32 UTC. Q: The calibration fill timing shows as -12.3ms on one line of the details panel and 12.3ms on the header and in the report. Which convention everywhere? Answer: chose "Positive = hit early (Recommended)"
- 23:32 UTC. Q: Song Details cuts the average multiplier off at 3 decimals; the report rounds it. Which one everywhere? Answer: chose "Round (Recommended)"
- 23:33 UTC. Q: Storing the squeezed-out note's tick and each activation's collected phrases means a new record format. Hydra's rule is that older records then read Stale until they're re-analyzed, which means re-analyzing the whole library once. Is that OK? Answer: chose "Yes, bump and re-analyze (Recommended)"
- 23:33 UTC. Q: hydra_replay's JSON reports each chord's multiplier one step behind the one it was paid at. fcvideo's analyze.py multiplies missed ghost/accent bonuses by that field, so on chords that cross 10/20/30 combo it underprices the loss. Fixing the replay changes fcvideo's numbers on those chords. Fix it? Answer: chose "Fix it (Recommended)"
- 23:33 UTC. Q: A plain backend row between 2 and 3 ms past the SP end: the table calls it 'Standard' and the engine counts it for free (3 ms leeway), but the rating treats anything past 2 ms as needing a late frontend hit and can raise the 'SP overfilled' warning. Which edge wins? Answer: chose "3 ms everywhere (Recommended)"
- 23:33 UTC. Q: The library scan only finds loose charts named exactly 'notes.mid' / 'song.ini' in lower case, while the parser accepts any case. Should the scan accept 'Notes.mid', 'Song.ini' and so on? Answer: chose "Accept any case (Recommended)"
- 23:34 UTC. Q: The Preview plays audio from 0 at chart time 0 and ignores song.ini 'delay' and the .chart 'Offset'. Test charts like Thornhill – Limbo (delay 1016) would play out of sync. What should happen? Answer: chose "Check the game, then honor (Recommended)"
- 23:34 UTC. Q: A song.ini with an empty 'name =' shows a blank title in the library but '(unknown)' in the report. Which one everywhere? Answer: chose ""(unknown)" everywhere (Recommended)"
- 23:34 UTC. Q: The Preview's time box size (15 and 10) is hard-coded, and 3d-config.json has the same values but is never read, so editing the JSON does nothing. Which way? Answer: chose "Read it from the JSON (Recommended)"
- 23:34 UTC. Q: The Dynamics tab's saved ghost/accent counts carry no version, so after a parser change they keep showing old counts. Add a version stamp so they're re-counted automatically? Answer: chose "Add a stamp (Recommended)"
- 23:36 UTC. Q: Several rules have no written reason: the 3 ms leeway, 'a squeeze-out removes only the first note's share', the auto-fill constants, the 4-tied-paths limit, the Auto cap ladder and its 120 s budget. How should the reasons be found? Pick any that apply. Answer: typed "these are all arbitrary rules i decided for my purposes. make them configurable in a  config file."
- 23:37 UTC. Q: Where should the rule values live? Answer: chose "New hydra_rules.ini (Recommended)"
- 23:37 UTC. Q: When a rule value changes, what happens to records analyzed under the old values? Answer: chose "Stamp + read Stale (Recommended)"
- 23:37 UTC. Q: The 'squeeze-out removes only the first note's share' rule is a rule, not a number. What choices should the setting offer? Answer: chose "first note / whole chord"
- 00:04 UTC. Q: fcvideo's multiplier_steps (video-tools/replay.py:182) was written around hydra_replay's current multiplier field. Its 'step is the chord before the field changes' rule only lands on the right chord because of the lag. If hydra_replay alone is fixed, fcvideo marks the disc change one chord early when a chord's first note is the 10th/20th/30th. How should this go? Answer: chose "Fix both repos (Recommended)"
- 00:04 UTC. Q: If hydra_rules.ini has a bad value or an unknown key, what should the GUI do? The CLI tools stop with an error naming the key either way. Answer: chose "Refuse to analyze (Recommended)"
- 00:04 UTC. Q: Decision 1 made an uncounted squeezed-out row show 0 points. The plain 'Hard (uncounted)' and 'Insane (uncounted)' backend rows still show their full points today. Should they also show 0, and should uncounted rows lose their colour highlight? Answer: chose "0 points, no highlight (Recommended)"
- 00:04 UTC. Q: The 1/32-beat slop that decides which chord an AUTHORED fill lands on (fill_lands_on_chord) isn't an auto-generated-fill constant. The audit lumped it in. Should it be a hydra_rules.ini value too? Answer: chose "Yes, configurable"
- 00:05 UTC. Q: The new version stamp on Dynamics counts: what should it track? Answer: chose "Parser counter (Recommended)"
- 00:05 UTC. Q: Empty song titles: decision 10 covers 'name =' with nothing after it. A song.ini with no name key at all, or a .srb with an empty name, shows '<unknown title>' today. Should those also show '(unknown)'? Answer: chose "Yes, '(unknown)' for all (Recommended)"
- 00:05 UTC. Q: In hydra_replay --acts, a typed SqOut offset is matched to the nearest phrase chord. What if that chord is one the engine could never squeeze out (for example, not the first phrase chord in the 500 ms window)? Answer: chose "Refuse with a message (Recommended)"
- 00:05 UTC. Q: squeeze_rating.cpp has three helpers (sp_end_shift_ms and two siblings) that only tests call. Their comment says so. Keep or delete? Answer: chose "Delete them and their tests (Recommended)"
- 00:07 UTC. Q: Three numbers have no recorded reason and aren't in your rules list: 8 batch workers (analysis.cpp:600), the Ch10 legacy-fill 1/16-beat pad (graph.cpp:58, ported from Python Hydra 1.2), and the multiplier-squeeze combo set 7/8/17/18/27/28 (model.cpp:338). What should happen to them? Answer: chose "Explain in code, keep fixed (Recommended)"
- 00:07 UTC. Q: Proof thresholds for the engine changes. Moving the engine onto the shared backend-pricing function may cost at most 5% search time (best of 3, on the largest test chart). Tracking collected phrases in the search may cost at most 3% on the hydra_batch run. Scores must be identical in both cases. OK? Answer: chose "5% and 3% are fine (Recommended)"
- 00:07 UTC. Q: Two small behavior edges came up. First, hydra_settings.ini's view_difficulty only matches exact case, so 'hard' silently falls back to Expert. - 00:33 UTC. Q: The video-tools repo has uncommitted edits in six files. One changes fcvideo's multiplier_steps from `i` to `i - 1`, and another adds a ninth check_refs case (wont_get_fooled_again). - 00:33 UTC. Q: `hydra_replay dump` explains a Stale record as 'was written by Hydra X, not this build'. After the rules fingerprint lands, a record can also be Stale because hydra_rules.ini changed. How should it say that? Answer: chose "Name the actual reason (Recommended)"
- 00:33 UTC. Q: video-tools' analyze.py prices a mis-hit ghost or accent at 50 × multiplier. The real loss is 65 × multiplier. No number changes either way. Fix the wording in Task 8 too? Answer: chose "Yes, fix it in Task 8 (Recommended)"

The plan's "User decisions" block, copied word for word when the plan left the repo (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-derivation-fixes.md` (removed; `git show 444631e6:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-derivation-fixes.md`)):

**User decisions (already made):**

1. The squeezed-out row the engine counts as 0 shows 0 in Points with the tag "squeezed out (uncounted)".
2. The engine's own search loop also calls the shared backend-pricing function, proven by an identical hydra_batch diff and a timing check.
3. Calibration fill sign: positive means hit early, everywhere.
4. The average multiplier is rounded (py_round3) everywhere, so Song Details changes.
5. Storing the squeezed-out tick and collected phrases is a new record format. Old records read Stale until re-analyzed, with no guessing fallback.
6. hydra_replay's JSON reports the multiplier category_scores actually applied: a per-note multiplier, and a `multiplier_after` on each chord.
7. For 2-3 ms plain backend rows the 3 ms leeway wins everywhere, in the rating and the cap_clamped flag too.
8. The library scan accepts any case for notes.mid, notes.chart and song.ini, through one helper shared with the parser.
9. Preview audio delay and Offset: first a live check at the game, then the Preview honors them.
10. A song with no usable name shows "(unknown)" everywhere: an empty `name =`, a missing name key, and an empty .srb or .sng name.
11. The Preview time box size is read from assets/preview/3d-config.json. Hydra keeps its own font for it.
12. Dynamics count rows get a stamp, a hand-bumped parser counter constant, so older counts are recomputed on next view.
13. The 3 ms leeway, the squeeze-out rule, the fill constants (including the 1/32-beat landing slop), 4 tied paths, the Auto cap ladder and its 120 s budget are your own choices and become configurable in hydra_rules.ini.
14. The file is a new hydra_rules.ini next to the exe, read by the GUI and every CLI tool through `--rules`.
15. Records store the rules fingerprint. A mismatch reads Stale until re-analyzed, and records come back when the rules are switched back.
16. The squeeze-out rule offers `first_note` (today) and `whole_chord` (every note in the chord loses its SP doubling).
17. Fix both repos: hydra_replay and video-tools' fcvideo (multiplier_steps, dynamics pricing), with check_refs staying green. The six uncommitted video-tools edits are committed as-is first.
18. A bad value or unknown key in hydra_rules.ini: the CLIs exit with code 2. The GUI opens, shows the error, and keeps Analyze disabled until the file is fixed.
19. Every backend row the engine does not count shows 0 points and no highlight, including plain "Hard (uncounted)" and "Insane (uncounted)". The label text stays.
20. A typed SqOut offset landing on a chord the engine would never squeeze out is refused, with a message naming that chord and the one the engine would pick.
21. The three test-only squeeze_rating helpers (sp_end_shift_ms, required_frontend_ms, exact_even_split_ms) and their tests are deleted.
22. The 8 batch workers, the Ch10 1/16-beat pad and the combo set 7/8/17/18/27/28 stay fixed, each with a code comment saying why (the combo set's reason is checked against to_multiplier first).
23. Speed limits: 5% for the backend function, 3% for the phrase bookkeeping. Past either, stop and report.
24. `view_difficulty` in the settings INI matches any case.
25. The MIDI reader follows mido on an over-long meta message, but keeps loading a track that is cut off mid-event, as it does today.
26. video-tools' dynamics pricing is fixed too: a mis-hit ghost or accent costs its own note's real bonus, from a new per-note field in the replay JSON.
27. `hydra_replay dump` names the actual reason a record is Stale: a different Hydra build, different rules, or both.
28. video-tools names each mis-hit ghost or accent from its own note (a per-note `dynamic` field), not from the chord. Only the loss text changes, and only on chords that mix a ghost and an accent.
